# Learn-To-Fly — v2 Master Plan

> **Status:** Planning · Not started. v2 begins after the v1.0 launch (v1.md Phase 13).
>
> **Scope of this document:** Every step from a launched v1 to a deployed **v2** of
> Learn-To-Fly. v2 keeps the **Cessna 172 Skyhawk** in **Microsoft Flight Simulator 2024
> (MSFS 2024)** and takes the learner far beyond the first cross-country: performance,
> crosswind mastery, weather, night, mountains, system failures, busy airspace, online ATC
> and the foundations of instrument flying. It also delivers the v1.1 backlog and the
> platform features that a multi-course product needs (courses, regions, a logbook, a
> practice hub).
>
> **Out of scope for v2:** Every aircraft other than the Cessna 172. The aircraft ladder
> toward the Airbus A380 starts in v3 (Section 56).
>
> **Companion document:** [`v1.md`](./v1.md) is the v1 master plan. v2 builds on it and
> does not repeat it. When this plan says "v1 Section 16" it means Section 16 of `v1.md`;
> a bare "Section 16" means Section 16 of this file.

---

## How to use this document

1. **Finish v1 first.** v2 assumes v1 Phases 0–13 are done and v1.0 is live. Anything
   left over from v1 that v2 needs is listed in Phase 15 (the v1.1 carry-over).
2. **Read Part I.** It defines what v2 _is_, what it is _not_, and the decisions that shape
   it (Section 5).
3. **Use Part II to learn the new flying yourself.** Night, mountain and instrument flying
   are big steps up from v1. Part II has the resources and an 11-week self-study path.
4. **Parts III–V are the specification.** They describe only what is **new or changed**
   compared with v1. Anything not mentioned here works exactly as v1 describes.
5. **Part VI is the execution plan.** Phases **14–26** continue v1's numbering (v1 used
   Phases 0–13), so a step ID such as "17.4" is unique across both plans.
6. **Part VII covers quality, risk, legal and the demo.** Read it before release.
7. **The appendices are reference material:** glossary additions, procedures in our own
   words, file templates, formulas and the condensed master checklist.

### Conventions used in this plan

All v1 conventions apply (v1 "Conventions used in this plan"). In short:

- `- [ ]` marks an actionable step. Tick it (`- [x]`) in the PR that completes it.
- **P0** = required for v2 release. **P1** = nice-to-have for v2; fine if it slips to v2.x.
  **P2** = explicitly post-v2.
- **AC** = Acceptance Criteria. **Est.** = rough part-time estimate.
- **⚠ Verify** marks an aviation fact, number, procedure or sim behaviour that you must
  check against the in-sim aircraft, the official handbook, a current chart or the current
  sim before publishing it. v2 has far more of these than v1, because IFR procedures,
  approach charts and sim failure menus change often.
- IDs that are new in v2 carry a `2` so they never clash with v1 IDs:
  decisions **D2-01…**, user stories **US2-01…**, milestones **M2-A…**, risks **R2-01…**.
  Lessons, challenges and widgets continue v1's numbering (Modules 9–21, widgets W21–W39)
  because they live in the same product.

### Document maintenance rules

- This file is the single source of truth for v2 planning. `v1.md` stays the source of
  truth for everything v1 built.
- If a v2 decision changes, update the **v2 Decision Log** (Section 5) _and_ the affected
  section. If a v2 change alters something v1 built, also add a short note to the v1
  section it overrides.
- Do not delete completed steps. Tick them, so the plan also becomes a build log.
- Every task = one PR (except if the task is e.g. a phase), same as v1.

# Part I — Product definition

## 1. Vision, mission and the v2 promise

### 1.1 Vision (unchanged)

Learn-To-Fly teaches complete beginners to fly _professionally_ in Microsoft Flight
Simulator 2024, starting in a **Cessna 172 Skyhawk** and ending in an **Airbus A380**,
with in-depth teaching and hundreds of in-sim challenges along the way (v1 Section 1.1).

### 1.2 Where v1 leaves the learner

A v1 graduate can set up the sim, fly the four fundamentals, take off, fly a pattern and
land, handle stalls and an engine failure, and fly a planned VFR cross-country in good
weather in the San Francisco Bay Area, with basic radio calls (v1 Section 1.2).

What they cannot yet do is everything that makes a pilot _well-rounded_: judge aircraft
performance on a hot day, land in a gusty crosswind, read a forecast and say "no", fly at
night, cross mountains, cope with a failed alternator or a frozen airspeed indicator,
talk to approach control in busy airspace, fly on an online ATC network, or keep the
airplane upright and on course in cloud.

### 1.3 Mission for v2

Go deep before going wide. Keep the Skyhawk and turn a v1 graduate into a pilot who can:

1. Calculate density altitude, takeoff and landing distance, weight and balance and fuel,
   and fly the airplane accordingly.
2. Take off and land in gusty crosswinds, on short and soft fields, and use slips.
3. Read real weather products, make a go/no-go decision and avoid VFR flight into cloud.
4. Fly safely at night, including lighting, illusions and night emergencies.
5. Plan and fly a mountain route into high-elevation airports.
6. Recognise and handle electrical, instrument, engine and fire emergencies.
7. Fly through Class B and Class C airspace, use flight following, and fly on VATSIM
   without embarrassment.
8. Fly the airplane solely by reference to instruments, including partial panel and
   unusual-attitude recovery.
9. Understand the IFR system: charts, clearances, departures, enroute, holds.
10. Fly RNAV (GPS), ILS and VOR approaches and missed approaches, hand-flown and with the
    autopilot, and complete an IFR cross-country capstone.

Along the way, v2 gives learners the tools real pilots use to stay sharp: a **logbook**,
**currency tracking**, **spaced-repetition practice** of memory items and a **kneeboard**.

### 1.4 The v2 promise (one sentence)

> "In about 55–65 more hours of lessons and sim time, Learn-To-Fly takes you from your first
> cross-country to a confident all-round Skyhawk pilot who can fly at night, into the
> mountains, through failures and down an instrument approach to minimums."

### 1.5 What makes v2 different from v1

- **Courses.** The product grows from one course to three: _Skyhawk Foundations_ (v1),
  _Skyhawk Advanced VFR_ and _Skyhawk Instrument Foundations_.
- **Decision-making is graded.** Diverting, going around and cancelling are scored as
  passes when they are the right call (Section 13.3).
- **Challenges feel different every time.** Scenario variants change the wind, the
  weather or the failure (Section 16.1).
- **Failures, the way instructors do them.** An event card tells you when and how to fail
  something, so failures work the same on every sim setup (D2-09).
- **Some criteria grade themselves.** Planning and drill challenges (weight and balance,
  go/no-go, clearance readbacks) are checked automatically — a first step toward full
  auto-grading.
- **Staying sharp.** A logbook, sim currency, and a daily practice queue of flashcards and
  drills.

### 1.6 What v2 is not

- It is still not real-world flight training and must never claim to be (v1 Section 57,
  Section 54 here). This matters even more for night, mountain and instrument content.
- It is not an instrument rating course. "Instrument Foundations" teaches the core skills
  and the system; it does not cover everything the Instrument Rating ACS requires
  (Section 2.3).
- It does not read data from the sim. Auto-grading of flights still needs the SimConnect
  companion (v1 Section 59.2), which stays an independent track (D2-04).
- MSFS 2024 is not an FAA-approved aviation training device. Time flown in Learn-To-Fly
  cannot be logged toward any certificate or currency. The v2 logbook is a habit tracker.

---

## 2. v2 scope: in, out, and later

### 2.1 In scope for v2 (P0 unless marked)

**Content**

- Two new courses in the same Cessna 172 (G1000 NXi primary, classic callouts):
  - **Course 2 — Skyhawk Advanced VFR:** Modules 9–16, 36 lessons (33 P0, 3 P1) and
    34 challenges (26 P0, 8 P1).
  - **Course 3 — Skyhawk Instrument Foundations:** Modules 17–21, 20 lessons (19 P0,
    1 P1) and 20 challenges (16 P0, 4 P1).
  - Totals new in v2: **56 lessons (52 P0, 4 P1)** and **54 challenges (42 P0, 12 P1)**.
    Full list in Section 14.
- The v1 P1 lessons and challenges (7 lessons, 6 challenges) become **P0** and ship in
  Phase 15, because Course 2 builds on them.
- A second region, the **Sierra Nevada (Lake Tahoe)**, for mountain flying, and an
  extended Bay Area (Central Coast and Central Valley airports) for night, IFR and longer
  cross-countries (Section 12).
- Weather scenario library (curated METAR/TAF/forecast sets) for go/no-go training.
- Approach plate explorer built on an **original training plate**, with links to the
  current FAA plates for every real procedure used (D2-10).
- Glossary grown to **≥ 300 terms**; **≥ 250 flashcards** across the practice decks.

**Product features**

- **Courses:** course catalog, course overview pages, per-course progress, stage checks
  and course badges.
- **Regions:** region pages, airports grouped by region, challenge filters by region.
- **Challenge engine v2:** scenario variants, typed events (failure cards, prompts,
  look-away cues), multi-leg challenges, auto-graded planning and drill criteria, stage
  checks, night and IFR metadata.
- **Logbook:** automatic entries from challenge attempts, manual entries, totals, CSV
  export.
- **Sim currency panel:** day, night and instrument "currency" modelled on 14 CFR 61.57,
  clearly labelled as a habit tracker.
- **Practice hub:** spaced-repetition flashcards (memory items, limits, signals,
  phraseology, chart symbols) and short drills built on widgets.
- **Kneeboard:** printable challenge briefs, nav logs, weight-and-balance forms and IFR
  approach briefing cards.
- **Tools hub:** crosswind, wind triangle, nav log (v1 P1), plus density altitude, weight
  and balance, performance, holding entry and descent planner.
- 13 new P0 widgets plus the 7 v1 P1 widgets (Section 17).
- v1.1 carry-over: password reset, email verification (P1), classic-panel mode, glossary
  hover-cards, "What's new", learning-signals admin page (Section 39).
- Dashboard v2: course switcher, currency, practice due, logbook totals, refresher
  suggestions.

**Engineering**

- Same MERN stack, same single deployable (v1 D-06), same content-as-files pipeline
  (v1 D-08). New collections and a small migration framework (Section 36).
- Transactional email provider for password reset and verification.
- Additive API changes only; `/api/v1` stays backward compatible (D2-14).

### 2.2 P1 — nice-to-have for v2 (ship if time allows, else v2.x)

- The 4 P1 lessons and 12 P1 challenges in Section 14.
- The 6 P1 widgets (W24, W26, W28, W29, W35, W39).
- Live real-world METAR on airport cards (Section 29.7).
- "Anywhere mode" for manoeuvre challenges (fly them at your own airport).
- Night-friendly (dim red) fly mode.
- Author verification dashboard (admin).
- Offline fly mode via a service worker; QR code to open fly mode on a phone.
- Logbook backfill from v1 attempts.
- Email verification.

### 2.3 Explicitly out of scope for v2 (P2 / post-v2)

- Any aircraft other than the Cessna 172 (the ladder starts in v3).
- The SimConnect companion and flight auto-grading (independent track, D2-04).
- A full instrument rating syllabus: DME arcs beyond awareness, NDB/ADF, SIDs and STARs
  beyond awareness, IFR in the classic (steam-gauge) panel.
- Commercial manoeuvres (chandelles, lazy eights, eights on pylons) — except the P1
  power-off 180 accuracy landing.
- Regions outside the US (UK, Alps) and unit switching (hPa, metres) — v1 D-13 stands.
- Localisation into languages other than English.
- Social features, leaderboards, comments, payments, native apps.
- An admin content-editing UI (content is still edited in the repo).
- Speech recognition for radio calls (the readback trainer uses typed answers).

### 2.4 Scope guardrails

v1's guardrails still apply (v1 Section 2.4). For v2, ask in order:

1. Does it help a v1 graduate become a well-rounded **Skyhawk** pilot? If no → post-v2.
2. Is it needed by a P0 lesson or challenge, or by the demo (Section 55)? If no → P1 at
   best.
3. Can it be done in under two days? If no → P1 at best, unless it blocks a P0 item.
4. Does it need data from the sim? If yes → post-v2 (companion track).
5. Write it in the parking lot (Section 56.5) and keep going.

## 3. Target users and personas

### 3.1 Primary persona — "Sam, the v1 graduate"

- Finished (or nearly finished) Course 1. Can land the Skyhawk consistently in calm air.
- **Goals:** "I want to fly at night and into the mountains, and I keep seeing people
  flying approaches in cloud — I want to do that too."
- **Frustrations:** Crosswinds; the jump from VFR to IFR looks huge; YouTube IFR tutorials
  assume knowledge Sam doesn't have.
- **Needs from v2:** a clear path, small steps, the same Brief → Fly → Debrief rhythm,
  and reasons to come back (practice, logbook, currency).

### 3.2 Secondary persona — "Alex, the student pilot"

- Now taking real lessons (v1's "aspiring real pilot"). Uses the sim between lessons.
- **Needs from v2:** accurate performance, weather and night content that matches what
  their instructor teaches; honest "Sim vs reality" callouts; ACS-inspired tolerances;
  memory-item practice.

### 3.3 New persona — "Riley, the rusty certificated pilot"

- Holds a private certificate, maybe an instrument rating, flies rarely. Uses the sim to
  rehearse procedures.
- **Needs from v2:** IFR procedures done properly (clearances, holds, approaches, missed
  approaches), G1000 NXi and GFC 700 workflow, the ability to skip what they already know.
- **Warning:** Riley is the most likely person to treat content as real guidance. IFR
  pages carry the strongest disclaimers (Section 54.1).

### 3.4 New persona — "Morgan, the online-ATC hopeful"

- Wants to fly on VATSIM but is afraid of sounding silly on frequency.
- **Needs from v2:** phraseology practice with feedback (readback trainer), a step-by-step
  VATSIM setup lesson, Class B/C procedures, and a safe first-flight plan.

### 3.5 Anti-persona (who v2 is not for)

- Anyone looking for FAA-creditable training time or an approved ground school.
- Airline simmers who only want the A320/737 today (v3+).
- Learners outside the US who need non-FAA rules and units (post-v2).

## 4. Success criteria and the definition of "v2 done"

### 4.1 Functional definition of done

v2 is done when **all** of the following are true:

- [ ] All P0 user stories in Section 3.6 pass their end-to-end test.
- [ ] Phase 15 (v1.1 carry-over) is complete, including the 7 v1 widgets and the 13 v1
      lessons/challenges that were P1.
- [ ] All 52 P0 v2 lessons are published, proofread and verified in-sim (Section 51).
- [ ] All 42 P0 v2 challenges are published and have been flown and passed by you at
      least once, following only the brief.
- [ ] All 13 P0 v2 widgets work with mouse, touch and keyboard.
- [ ] Logbook, currency panel, practice hub, tools hub and kneeboard are live.
- [ ] Production migrations have run with zero data loss (record counts before and after,
      Section 36.4).
- [ ] The v2 demo script (Section 55) runs start to finish without errors.
- [ ] The v2 release QA checklist (Section 52) is fully ticked.

### 4.2 Quality bars

All v1 quality bars still apply (v1 Section 4.2). v2 adds:

| Area                                            | Target                                 |
| ----------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
| Interaction to Next Paint (lesson with widgets) | < 200 ms (p75, mid-range phone)        |
| Initial JavaScript (gzipped)                    | still < 250 KB (new features are lazy) |
| Widget chunk (gzipped)                          | ≤ 60 KB each (guideline, as v1)        |
| `GET /me/logbook` p95 with 1,000 entries        | < 300 ms                               |
| `GET /me/practice/due` p95                      | < 200 ms                               |
| Migration dry run on a copy of production       | 0 errors, counts match                 |
| Content validation (`--strict`)                 | 0 errors, 0 warnings                   |
| Glossary / flashcards                           | ≥ 300 terms / ≥ 250 cards              |
| Server / client coverage                        | ≥ 80% / ≥ 70% (unchanged)              |

### 4.3 Learning success signals (to watch after release)

- Percentage of Course 1 graduates who start Course 2 within 30 days.
- Stage check pass rates and median attempts (C16.1, C21.1).
- Decision challenges: share of learners who divert or go around when the scenario calls
  for it (C11.2, C11.3, C13.4).
- Practice retention: share of reviews answered "Good" or "Easy" after 30 days.
- Median attempts before passing each IFR approach challenge (≥ 5 → rework the brief or
  the lesson).
- Weekly returning learners with at least one practice session (a proxy for habit).

### 4.4 "Show someone" readiness

- A v1 graduate can find Course 2 from the dashboard in one click and start Module 9.
- You can give a 15-minute demo (Section 55) that shows a course page, an auto-graded
  planning challenge, a failure card firing, the logbook and currency panel, a practice
  session, the approach plate explorer, and an approach flown in the sim.
- Nothing says "Lorem ipsum", "TODO" or "Coming soon" except the roadmap teaser.

---

## 5. Decision log (v2)

Format as v1 Section 5. These are the initial v2 decisions; revisit them, but never
silently.

### D2-01 — Still one aircraft: depth in the Cessna 172 before breadth

- **Alternatives:** Start the aircraft ladder (a complex single or a light twin) in v2.
- **Reason:** A v1 graduate is not yet a well-rounded pilot. Night, weather, mountains,
  failures and instrument flying are all learnable in the Skyhawk, reuse every piece of
  v1 reference data, and are prerequisites for every later aircraft. The ladder starts in
  v3 (Section 56).

### D2-02 — Three courses: Foundations, Advanced VFR, Instrument Foundations

- **Alternatives:** One ever-growing course; a separate site per course.
- **Reason:** A course is the right unit for goals, badges and stage checks, and it is
  what v1 Section 59.4 planned (`courses` collection, `courseSlug` fields). v1's Modules
  0–8 become **Course 1 — Skyhawk Foundations** without any slug or URL change.

### D2-03 — The v1.1 backlog is folded into v2 as Phase 15

- **Alternatives:** Ship v1.1 separately before starting v2.
- **Reason:** Course 2 depends on v1's P1 lessons (crosswind, short field, METARs,
  flight following) and P1 widgets (W13, W15, W17, W20). Doing them first, as a phase of
  v2, keeps one plan and one release train. Phase 15 can still be released on its own as
  `v1.1.0` (Section 37.3).

### D2-04 — The SimConnect companion stays an independent track

- **Alternatives:** Make the companion part of v2.
- **Reason:** It is a separate Windows project with its own install and support burden
  (v1 D-04), and it doesn't help Xbox users. v2 is designed to work **with or without**
  it: every v2 challenge criterion that could be measured from sim data gets an optional
  machine-readable `telemetry` spec (Section 27.4), so auto-grading can switch on later
  without rewriting content.

### D2-05 — Regions: extend the Bay Area, add the Sierra Nevada; stay in the US

- **Alternatives:** Seattle, Southern California, the UK, the Alps.
- **Reason:** The Sierra (Truckee, Lake Tahoe) is the nearest serious mountain flying to
  the v1 home base — a Bay-to-mountains cross-country becomes the Course 2 capstone. The
  Bay Area already has every airspace class, towered and non-towered fields, marine-layer
  weather, and ILS/RNAV/VOR approaches. Staying in the US keeps one rulebook (FAA) and one
  set of units (v1 D-13). The region system is built generically so more regions are
  content-only work later.

### D2-06 — Instrument Foundations, not an instrument rating

- **Alternatives:** A complete instrument rating syllabus.
- **Reason:** A complete syllabus would double v2. Foundations covers attitude instrument
  flying, partial panel, the IFR system, holds, RNAV/ILS/VOR approaches, missed
  approaches and an IFR cross-country. DME arcs, circling and procedure turns are P1;
  NDBs, SIDs/STARs and classic-panel IFR are post-v2. The course says so up front.

### D2-07 — IFR is taught on the G1000 NXi and GFC 700 only

- **Alternatives:** Teach IFR on both panels.
- **Reason:** The G1000 NXi variant has the GPS, the HSI and the autopilot the course
  needs, and it is the default most learners fly (v1 D-02). Partial-panel lessons use the
  G1000's standby instruments. Classic-panel IFR joins the parking lot.

### D2-08 — Decisions are scored as outcomes

- **Alternatives:** Only grade stick-and-rudder precision.
- **Reason:** The most important skills in v2 are judgement calls. Challenges such as the
  marine-layer diversion (C11.2) or mountain wave (C13.4) pass when the learner makes the
  safe decision early, even if they never reach the destination. Criteria of kind
  `decision` make this explicit (Section 16.2).

### D2-09 — Failures are simulated the way instructors do it

- **Alternatives:** Rely on the MSFS 2024 failures menu for every failure.
- **Reason:** Sim failure menus differ between versions and platforms and change with sim
  updates. Real instructors simulate most failures with a switch or knob ("alternator
  off", "mixture back to simulate rough running", "cover the PFD"). A challenge **event
  card** tells the learner exactly what to do at a random time. Where the sim's failure
  system is available and verified, the brief offers it as an optional "realism mode".

### D2-10 — Approach plates: an original training plate plus links to current FAA plates

- **Alternatives:** Embed crops of real FAA plates in lessons and widgets.
- **Reason:** FAA plates are public domain but change every 28/56 days; an embedded crop
  would be out of date and could be mistaken for current. W38 uses an **original,
  fictional "Learn-To-Fly Training Plate"** that is clearly labelled as not real. Every
  challenge that flies a real procedure links to the current FAA d-TPP PDF and states the
  cycle it was verified against.

### D2-11 — Performance numbers come from our own sim tests

- **Alternatives:** Copy the POH performance tables.
- **Reason:** POH tables are copyrighted (v1 Section 6.1) and the sim's airplane may not
  match them. As with the W5 table (v1 D-18), W22 and the performance lessons use tables
  built from our own flight tests in the sim, formatted like a POH chart and labelled
  "Learn-To-Fly sim data — use your POH in a real airplane". They ship provisional until
  flown.

### D2-12 — The logbook is a habit tracker, not a legal logbook

- **Alternatives:** No logbook; a free-form notes page.
- **Reason:** Logging and currency are real professional habits and give learners a
  reason to return. Every logbook and currency screen says "Sim flights do not count
  toward real-world currency or experience."

### D2-13 — Spaced repetition with a small SM-2-style scheduler in `shared/`

- **Alternatives:** A third-party flashcard library or service.
- **Reason:** The algorithm is tiny, easy to test and keeps data in our database. Memory
  items and IFR rules need retention, not one-off quizzes.

### D2-14 — API stays `/api/v1`, changes are additive

- **Alternatives:** Introduce `/api/v2`.
- **Reason:** The client and server deploy together (v1 D-06), so there are no old
  clients to support beyond one page refresh. New fields are optional; no field is
  removed or renamed.

### D2-15 — Transactional email via a provider (Resend or Postmark)

- **Alternatives:** SMTP from a personal mailbox; no email.
- **Reason:** Password reset is now P0 (US2-14). A provider handles deliverability, SPF
  and DKIM. Email is used **only** for account security messages — no marketing
  (Section 32.2).

### D2-16 — Content IDs keep counting; slugs never change

- **Alternatives:** Restart numbering in each course.
- **Reason:** Modules 9–21, lessons L9.1…, challenges C9.1…, widgets W21… stay globally
  unique, so every display code in the product, the plans and the PRs means one thing.
  v1 slugs and URLs are untouched (v1 Section 13.4).

### D2-17 — Content still goes live module by module

- **Alternatives:** Hold all v2 content until the v2.0.0 tag.
- **Reason:** v1 D-17 already publishes only verified content to production. Verified
  Course 2 modules can go live as soon as they are ready; the `v2.0.0` tag is cut when the
  definition of done (Section 4.1) is met.

---

# Part II — Learning advanced Skyhawk flying (for you, the author)

Same two purposes as v1 Part II: it is **your** study guide (you must be able to fly
every v2 challenge to Gold before you write it), and it is the **master list of
references** that v2 lessons link to. Every new resource is added to
`content/resources.yaml` with a `verifiedAt` date (v1 Section 6.1 rules apply unchanged).

## 7. Author self-study path for v2

Goal: in about **11 weeks at ~6–8 hours/week**, be able to fly every P0 v2 challenge to
Gold. It runs **in parallel** with Phases 15–19. Finish week N before writing the lessons
for the matching module (Phases 22–23). Keep using your study logbook (v1 Section 7.7) —
it becomes the "Common mistakes" source again.

### 7.1 Week 1 — Performance, weight and balance, leaning (Module 9)

- [ ] Read the PHAK chapters on weight and balance and aircraft performance.
- [ ] Record the sim's empty weight, empty CG and station arms (Section 8.2) from the sim's
      weight-and-balance panel.
- [ ] **Performance flight tests** (feeds W22, D2-11): at KLVK with `LOAD_SOLO` and
      `LOAD_FULL`, measure ground roll and distance to clear 50 ft at three density
      altitudes (sea-level standard day, `WX_HOT`, and a high airport in Week 5). Repeat
      each three times; record the average.
- [ ] Measure landing ground roll at 65 KIAS (normal) and 61 KIAS (short field) with
      flaps 30.
- [ ] Leaning: at 6,500 ft find peak EGT and record fuel flow and TAS at peak, 50 °F rich
      of peak, and full rich (feeds W24 and C9.3).

### 7.2 Week 2 — Advanced takeoffs and landings (Module 10)

- [ ] Read the AFH chapters on takeoffs and on approaches and landings (crosswind,
      slips, short and soft field).
- [ ] Ten crosswind landings at each of 8, 12 and 15 kt (use `WX_XWIND_*` and
      `WX_GUST_XW`), wing-low method.
- [ ] Forward slips from 1,000 ft AGL, flaps up, to a spot landing.
- [ ] Soft-field and short-field takeoffs and landings (five each).
- [ ] Find out how the sim models runway surfaces: is there a noticeable difference on
      grass? Find a grass strip in the region the sim renders well (⚠ record it).
- [ ] P1: power-off 180 accuracy landings.

### 7.3 Week 3 — Weather (Module 11)

- [ ] Read the Aviation Weather Handbook sections on air masses, fronts, stability, fog,
      thunderstorms, icing, and on METAR/TAF/GFA products.
- [ ] Every day this week, read the real KLVK/KHAF/KMRY METARs and TAFs and the NWS Area
      Forecast Discussion for the Bay Area. Write down whether you would fly and why.
- [ ] In the sim: build each v2 weather preset (Section 16.1) and fly in it. Find out what
      the sim does with cloud bases, visibility, gusts and turbulence (Section 9.3).
- [ ] Deliberately fly into a cloud layer with the Training profile and practise the 180°
      turn on instruments (C11.3). Time how long it takes to feel disoriented.

### 7.4 Week 4 — Night (Module 12)

- [ ] Read the AFH night operations chapter and the PHAK aeromedical chapter (vision).
- [ ] Fly at night at KLVK, KTCY and KWVI. Record which lights the sim models (runway
      edge, REIL, approach lights, PAPI/VASI, taxiway lights, beacon) and whether
      pilot-controlled lighting works (Section 9.2).
- [ ] Three night full-stop landings at KLVK; one night cross-country KLVK → KWVI → KLVK.
- [ ] Try the black-hole approach (KWVI or a coastal field over dark water) and note how it
      looks — it becomes a W28 example and an L12.1 screenshot.

### 7.5 Week 5 — Mountains and the Sierra region (Module 13)

- [ ] Read "Tips on Mountain Flying" and the AIM mountain flying section.
- [ ] Plan and fly KAUN → Donner Pass → KTRK, and KTRK → KTVL, in calm weather.
- [ ] Repeat on a hot afternoon (`WX_HOT_SIERRA`) with `LOAD_FULL`: note the climb
      rate and takeoff distance at Truckee (feeds W22 high-DA rows).
- [ ] Fly in `WX_MOUNTAIN_WAVE` and note what the sim shows (downdrafts on the lee side,
      turbulence, any rotor). Decide whether C13.4 is realistic enough to keep.
- [ ] Verify every Sierra airport and landmark in Section 12.3 in the sim's scenery.

### 7.6 Week 6 — Systems and failures (Module 14)

- [ ] Read the PHAK systems and instruments chapters and the AFH emergency chapter.
- [ ] Map the sim's G1000 NXi electrical system: what fails when the alternator is off?
      How long does the battery last? What does the standby battery power? (Section 8.4).
- [ ] Try every simulated failure in Section 9.1: alternator off, PFD reversion, pitot
      blockage (sim failure menu if available, otherwise a documented workaround), rough
      running via mixture, engine fire (event card only).
- [ ] Fly each failure scenario in C14.1–C14.5 at least twice.

### 7.7 Week 7 — Airspace, ATC and online flying (Module 15)

- [ ] Read AIM Chapter 3 (Class B/C, special VFR) and Chapter 4 (flight following,
      light gun signals, readbacks).
- [ ] Listen to NorCal Approach on LiveATC for an hour. Transcribe ten VFR exchanges —
      they become W32 readback items (paraphrased, not recorded audio).
- [ ] Fly a Class B transition and a flight-following flight with the sim's ATC; note
      differences from the AIM.
- [ ] Create a VATSIM account, complete any required onboarding (⚠ verify current
      requirements), install the pilot client, and observe (listen only) for a session.
      Then fly one VFR pattern on VATSIM. Take notes for L15.5.

### 7.8 Week 8 — Attitude instrument flying (Module 17)

- [ ] Read the IFH chapters on human factors, flight instruments and attitude instrument
      flying (airplane).
- [ ] In `WX_IMC_HIGH`, fly straight-and-level, standard-rate turns, constant-airspeed and
      constant-rate climbs and descents, and timed turns — to ±100 ft, ±10°, ±10 kt.
- [ ] Fly Pattern A and Pattern B (IFH/common training patterns; ⚠ verify the version you
      teach) with a stopwatch.
- [ ] Unusual attitudes using the look-away method (Section 16.1) and partial panel on the
      standby instruments.

### 7.9 Week 9 — The IFR system and holding (Modules 18–19)

- [ ] Read the IPH departure, enroute and holding chapters and AIM Chapter 5.
- [ ] Download the current d-TPP pages for every airport in Section 12.4 and one IFR low
      chart covering the Bay Area.
- [ ] Program full IFR flight plans in the G1000 (departure, airways if available,
      arrival, approach) and fly them on the autopilot.
- [ ] Fly holds at a VOR and at a GPS waypoint by hand, all three entries, with wind.

### 7.10 Week 10 — Approaches (Module 20)

- [ ] Read the IPH approach chapter and the approach sections of the G1000 NXi guide.
- [ ] Fly every candidate approach in Section 12.4 hand-flown and coupled; keep only the
      ones that exist in both the current d-TPP **and** the sim's nav data.
- [ ] Fly each to minimums in `WX_IMC_LOW` / `WX_IMC_NP`, and fly each missed approach to
      the hold.

### 7.11 Week 11 — IFR capstone and review (Module 21)

- [ ] Plan and fly C21.2 end to end, including a real-looking clearance, the alternate
      decision and the fuel check.
- [ ] Fly C16.1 and C21.1 stage checks from the brief only.
- [ ] Write your reflection: which v2 skills were hardest? Those lessons get the most
      widgets and screenshots.

## 8. Cessna 172S reference data additions

> ⚠ **Every number in this section must be verified** against the MSFS 2024 aircraft, its
> in-sim checklist and, if available, the Cessna 172S POH. These are widely published
> values for the 172S and are a planning baseline only. All of it goes into
> `content/aircraft.yaml` so lessons use tokens, never literals.

| Short-field approach speed (flaps 30) | 61 KIAS                                                        | `{{vspeed.shortFinal}}`          |
| Soft-field lift-off                   | As soon as possible, then accelerate in ground effect to Vx/Vy | —                                |
| Best glide (2,550 lb)                 | 68 KIAS (v1)                                                   | `{{vspeed.glide}}`               |

| Service ceiling                       | 14,000 ft                                                      | `{{limit.serviceCeiling}}`       |
| Maximum takeoff / landing weight      | 2,550 lb                                                       | `{{limit.mtow}}`                 |

| Station                 | Arm (in aft of datum, ⚠ verify) | Notes                                        |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| Front seats             | 37 (adjustable 34–46)           | Pilot and front passenger                    |
| Rear seats              | 73                              |                                              |

- **CG envelope (normal category, ⚠ verify):** forward limit 35.0 in at 1,950 lb or less,
  rising linearly to 41.0 in at 2,550 lb; aft limit 47.3 in at all weights.
- W23 draws this envelope from `aircraft.yaml → weightAndBalance.envelope` (a list of
  weight/arm points), so fixing the numbers never touches code.

### 8.3 Performance tables (built from sim tests, D2-11)

Stored in `aircraft.yaml → performanceTables` with `verified: false` until flown.

| Table                         | Axes                                                        | Source                    |
| ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| Takeoff ground roll / 50 ft   | pressure altitude 0–8,000 ft × temperature 0–40 °C × weight | Week 1 and 5 flight tests |
| Landing ground roll / 50 ft   | pressure altitude × temperature (weight 2,550)              | Week 1 flight tests       |
| Rate of climb at Vy           | pressure altitude × temperature                             | Week 1 and 5 flight tests |
| Cruise (RPM → TAS, fuel flow) | altitude 2,000–10,000 ft × RPM 2,100–2,500                  | Week 1 leaning tests      |

Corrections taught (⚠ verify against the POH notes, and label as typical): headwind
reduces distance (roughly 10% per 9 kt), tailwind increases it (roughly 10% per 2 kt),
dry grass adds roughly 15% of ground roll.

### 8.4 Systems detail for failures (Module 14)

| System              | What v2 needs (⚠ verify every item in the sim and the POH)                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Electrical          | 28 V system, 24 V main battery, 60 A alternator; ALT and BAT master halves; ammeter/load display on the EIS; "LOW VOLTS" annunciation; essential and avionics buses; standby battery for the standby instruments and how long it lasts |
| Pitot-static        | Heated pitot (PITOT HEAT switch); static port(s); **alternate static source** valve (location in the sim cockpit); which G1000 readouts depend on the ADC                                                                              |
| AHRS/ADC            | What the G1000 shows when AHRS or ADC fails (red X), and which standby instruments remain                                                                                                                                              |
| Displays            | Reversionary mode: the **DISPLAY BACKUP** button and automatic reversion when one display fails                                                                                                                                        |
| Standby instruments | Standby attitude (electric or vacuum in the sim variant?), standby airspeed, standby altimeter, magnetic compass                                                                                                                       |
| Engine and fuel     | Fuel-injected (no carburettor heat on the 172S); fuel selector BOTH/LEFT/RIGHT; auxiliary fuel pump; fuel flow and EGT on the EIS. If the sim's classic variant is carbureted, add carb-ice notes to "Classic panel" callouts          |
| Lighting            | Beacon, strobe, NAV (position), landing, taxi lights; panel and flood lighting dimmers                                                                                                                                                 |

### 8.5 IFR equipment (G1000 NXi + GFC 700)

- **Nav sources:** GPS (WAAS), NAV1/NAV2 (VOR/LOC/GS), DME (⚠ does the sim's aircraft
  have DME?), marker beacons (⚠ verify).
- **CDI scaling (GPS):** ENR, TERM, LNAV/LPV approach modes with automatic transition —
  numbers in Appendix F.6 (⚠ verify).
- **Autopilot modes:** HDG, NAV, APR, ALT, VS, FLC, VNV, GA, and the yaw damper (if any).
  Record which ones the sim models and how "GA" and missed-approach sequencing behave.
- **Approach capability:** LPV, LNAV/VNAV, LNAV, ILS, LOC, VOR — whatever the sim's nav
  database and the G1000 support. Record it in `aircraft.yaml → ifrCapabilities`.
- **Timer:** the G1000 timer (for holds, timed approaches and Pattern A/B).

## 9. MSFS 2024 for v2: failures, night, weather, networks and IFR

As in v1, lessons describe the **intent** of each sim setting and give the current menu
path with a "last verified" date, because menus move between sim updates.

### 9.1 Failures

- [ ] ⚠ **Verify** what MSFS 2024 offers today: a failures panel, per-system failures
      (electrical, pitot/static, vacuum, instruments, engine), timed or random failures,
      and whether this works on Xbox.
- **Default method (D2-09):** instructor-style simulated failures, cued by an event card:

| Failure (v2)         | Instructor-style simulation                                                                            | Optional sim-failure "realism mode" (⚠ verify) |
| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------- |
| Alternator failure   | Turn the ALT half of the master switch off                                                             | Electrical → alternator                        |
| PFD failure          | Press DISPLAY BACKUP, then treat the PFD as failed (or cover it)                                       | Avionics → PFD                                 |
| AHRS / attitude loss | Cover the attitude indicator (sticky note) or use partial-panel view                                   | Instruments → AHRS/attitude                    |
| Pitot blockage       | ⚠ sim failure menu only; fallback: learner ignores airspeed and flies pitch/power with the ASI covered | Pitot-static → pitot                           |
| Static blockage      | ⚠ sim failure menu only; fallback: talk-through scenario card (P1)                                     | Pitot-static → static                          |
| Rough running engine | Pull the mixture back until rough (never to cut-off)                                                   | Engine → partial power                         |
| Partial power loss   | Reduce throttle to the card's RPM and do not touch it again                                            | Engine → power loss                            |
| Engine fire          | Event card only (the sim may not model fire); fly the memory items and forced landing                  | —                                              |
| Radio failure        | Turn the COM volume down / stop using the radio; squawk 7600 per the card                              | Avionics → COM                                 |

### 9.2 Night

- [ ] ⚠ Verify: time-of-day setting and how "night" is chosen in free flight; moon phase
      and moonlight; city lights in the Bay Area and the Sierra; which airport lights are
      modelled (edge, REIL, approach lights, PAPI/VASI, taxiway, beacons); whether
      **pilot-controlled lighting** works (clicking the CTAF frequency) and at which
      airports; cockpit lighting controls.
- If PCL is not modelled, C12.2 keeps the click procedure as a habit step and notes the
  difference in a "Sim vs reality" callout.

### 9.3 Weather

- [ ] ⚠ Verify: custom cloud layers (base, top, coverage), visibility and precipitation,
      wind layers and gusts, turbulence and thermals, and how **live weather** is sourced.
      Check how precisely the sim holds a cloud base (C11.3 and every IMC preset depend
      on it).
- All v2 challenges still specify custom weather for repeatability (v1 Section 9.5),
  except the P1 live-weather challenge (C11.4).

### 9.4 ATC: sim ATC, VATSIM, PilotEdge

- [ ] ⚠ Verify the sim ATC's support for: VFR flight following, Class B clearances, IFR
      clearances (with or without a filed flight plan), vectors to final, approach
      clearances, holds and missed approaches.
- Every communication challenge supports three modes (Section 16.1): **sim ATC**,
  **online network** (VATSIM/PilotEdge), and **self-voiced script** (the learner says both
  sides out loud using the brief's script). Criteria are written so all three can pass.
- Online networks are always optional (they depend on controller coverage and time of day)
  and PC-only (⚠ verify for Xbox).

### 9.5 IFR in the sim

- [ ] ⚠ Verify: the sim's nav database AIRAC cycle and whether procedures match the
      current d-TPP; how to load departures, approaches and holds in the G1000; the
      autopilot's approach behaviour (LPV glidepath capture, ILS capture, missed-approach
      sequencing); how to disable any "approach guidance" assistance.
- Navigraph (paid) can update the sim's nav data; never require it. If a procedure differs
  between the sim and the current d-TPP, teach the current one and fly the sim's in the
  challenge (the v1 Section 54.1 rule for airport data).
- **The "hood":** in the sim, IMC weather _is_ the view-limiting device. Challenges that
  need "instruments only" in clear weather (e.g. Pattern A over familiar terrain) ask the
  learner to use an instruments-only camera view (⚠ verify a suitable view exists) or
  set a solid overcast layer above the start altitude.

### 9.6 Assistance profile changes for v2

v1's Training profile (v1 Section 9.3) still applies. v2 adds:

| Setting (intent)                     | Recommended                         | Why                                             |
| ------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- |
| Failures / random failures           | Off unless the brief says otherwise | Surprise failures come from event cards (D2-09) |
| Approach/landing guidance aids       | Off                                 | Fly the needles, not the sim's guidance         |
| Autopilot assistance                 | Off                                 | You manage the modes                            |
| Weather: live                        | Off (custom weather per brief)      | Repeatability                                   |
| Cockpit lighting / night vision aids | Realistic                           | Night lessons depend on it                      |
| Crash damage                         | On                                  | As v1 from Module 4                             |

## 10. Weather, night and mountain flying reference

The knowledge base for Modules 11–13. Each subsection maps to a lesson. ⚠ Verify every
rule and number against the sources in Section 6 before publishing.

### 10.1 Weather theory essentials (L11.1)

- **Air masses** take the properties of where they form (maritime/continental,
  polar/tropical). The Bay Area's summer pattern: cool maritime air at the coast, hot
  continental air inland — the sea-breeze and the marine layer come from that contrast.
- **Fronts:** cold (narrow band, gusty, showers, rapid clearing), warm (wide area of low
  cloud and poor visibility ahead of it), stationary and occluded.
- **Stability:** stable air → stratiform clouds, poor visibility, smooth air; unstable
  air → cumuliform clouds, good visibility outside showers, turbulence.
- **Temperature/dewpoint spread** closing below about 3 °C → fog or low cloud likely.

### 10.2 Products (L11.2)

| Product                           | What it answers                                        | Taught how                   |
| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------- |
| METAR / SPECI                     | What is it like now?                                   | W17 (v1 P1) with v2 examples |
| TAF                               | What will it be like at this airport (5 sm radius)?    | W17 TAF mode                 |
| GFA                               | Clouds, weather, winds, icing, turbulence by area      | Annotated screenshots        |
| Winds and temperatures aloft (FB) | Winds for planning and mountain crossings              | Decoding table + W12 link    |
| AIRMET (Sierra, Tango, Zulu)      | Widespread IFR/mountain obscuration, turbulence, icing | Table                        |
| SIGMET / Convective SIGMET        | Severe hazards                                         | Table                        |
| PIREP                             | What pilots actually found                             | Decoding table               |

### 10.3 VFR into IMC and the 180° turn (L11.6, C11.3)

- VFR into IMC is one of the leading causes of fatal general aviation accidents
  (⚠ cite a current AOPA ASI / NTSB source in the lesson).
- The escape: **trust the instruments, level the wings, a standard-rate or shallower turn
  (about 15–20° bank) through 180° to the reciprocal heading, hold altitude, ask for
  help.** Rehearsed in C11.3 until it is automatic.

### 10.4 Night rules and facts (Module 12)

| Topic                               | Plain-language content (⚠ verify each against 14 CFR / AIM)                                                                     |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Position lights (91.209)            | Required from sunset to sunrise                                                                                                 |
| "Night" for logging (61.51 / 1.1)   | End of evening civil twilight to beginning of morning civil twilight                                                            |
| Night passenger currency (61.57(b)) | Three takeoffs and landings to a full stop, in the preceding 90 days, between one hour after sunset and one hour before sunrise |
| VFR fuel reserve (91.151)           | 30 min day, **45 min night**                                                                                                    |
| Pilot-controlled lighting (AIM)     | Key the mic 7 times in 5 s for high, 5 for medium, 3 for low                                                                    |
| Night vision                        | Rods vs cones, ~30 min dark adaptation, off-centre viewing, red light trade-offs                                                |
| Illusions                           | Black-hole approach, autokinesis, false horizon, featureless terrain                                                            |

### 10.5 Mountain flying essentials (Module 13)

- **Density altitude** dominates: less power, less lift, longer runs, worse climb. Lean
  for best power before takeoff at high airports (⚠ verify the POH/in-sim method, e.g.
  leaning for maximum RPM at full throttle during the run-up).
- **Wind:** air flowing over a ridge rises on the windward side and sinks — sometimes
  violently — on the lee side. Strong winds at ridge level bring mountain wave, rotors and
  turbulence. A commonly taught caution: winds at ridge level above about 20–25 kt → don't
  go (⚠ verify the wording in "Tips on Mountain Flying").
- **Crossing ridges:** plan to cross with at least 1,000–2,000 ft clearance, approach at
  a 45° angle so you can turn away toward lower terrain, and know your escape route.
- **Passes and valleys:** fly on the side of the valley that gives you room to turn;
  never enter a canyon you cannot turn around in.
- **Time of day:** early morning is calmer and cooler; afternoons bring higher DA and
  convection over the Sierra in summer.

---

## 11. Instrument flying reference

The knowledge base for Modules 17–21. ⚠ Verify every rule against the IFH, IPH, AIM and
14 CFR before publishing; teach simplified tables with links to the source.

### 11.1 Attitude instrument flying

- **Control and performance method:** set attitude and power (control instruments:
  attitude indicator, tachometer); check the result on the performance instruments
  (altimeter, airspeed, VSI, heading, turn rate); adjust; trim.
- **Scan:** a radial scan centred on the attitude indicator; avoid **fixation**,
  **omission** and **emphasis** errors.
- **Primary/supporting** (for reference): the instrument that gives the most direct
  information for a given parameter is primary (e.g. altimeter for pitch in level flight).
- **Standard-rate turn:** 3°/s; bank ≈ TAS ÷ 10 + 7 (v1 Appendix G.5). Timed turns:
  15 s per 45°.

### 11.2 IFR rules (Course 3 tables)

| Topic                            | Plain-language content (⚠ verify)                                                                                                 |
| -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| IFR fuel (91.167)                | Fly to the destination, then to the alternate (if required), then 45 minutes at normal cruise                                     |
| Alternate required? (91.169)     | "1-2-3 rule": no alternate needed if, from 1 h before to 1 h after ETA, ceiling ≥ 2,000 ft and visibility ≥ 3 sm                  |
| Alternate minimums               | Standard: precision 600 ft and 2 sm, non-precision 800 ft and 2 sm, unless the plate says otherwise (⚠ verify)                    |
| Instrument currency (61.57(c))   | Within the preceding 6 calendar months: 6 instrument approaches, holding procedures and tasks, intercepting and tracking courses  |
| Lost comms (91.185)              | Route: Assigned, Vectored, Expected, Filed (AVEF). Altitude: highest of assigned, MEA, or expected (for the segment). Squawk 7600 |
| Descending below DA/MDA (91.175) | Only with the required visibility and runway environment in sight, from a normal descent to a normal landing                      |
| Clearance format                 | **CRAFT:** Clearance limit, Route, Altitude, Frequency, Transponder                                                               |

### 11.3 Holding

- Standard holds use **right turns**; non-standard use left. Inbound leg **1 minute** at
  or below 14,000 ft MSL.
- **Entries** (AIM): direct, teardrop, parallel — determined by the heading on arrival at
  the fix relative to the inbound course (sector formula in Appendix F.5). Teach "pick the
  entry, then fly it; any safe entry is better than a late one" — the ACS standard is the
  recommended entry.
- **Timing and wind:** start timing outbound abeam the fix; adjust the outbound time to
  make the inbound leg 1 minute; triple the inbound wind correction on the outbound leg.
- **The 5 Ts** at every fix: Turn, Time, Twist, Throttle, Talk.

### 11.4 Approaches

| Type      | Guidance                         | Minimum | Notes                                                           |
| --------- | -------------------------------- | ------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ILS       | Localizer + glideslope           | DA      | Precision approach; CDI very sensitive near the runway          |
| LPV       | WAAS GPS lateral + vertical      | DA      | Flies like an ILS; "APV" (approach with vertical guidance)      |
| LNAV/VNAV | GPS lateral + baro/WAAS vertical | DA      | Temperature limits may apply (⚠ verify)                         |
| LNAV      | GPS lateral only                 | MDA     | Non-precision; step-downs; may show advisory glidepath (LNAV+V) |
| LOC       | Localizer only                   | MDA     | Non-precision                                                   |
| VOR       | VOR radial                       | MDA     | Non-precision; often not aligned with the runway                |

- **Segments:** initial (IAF), intermediate (IF), final (FAF → MAP), missed approach.
- **Continuous descent final approach (CDFA)** is taught as the default for non-precision
  approaches; "dive and drive" is explained as the older technique.
- **Missed approach:** at the DA or MAP without the runway environment in sight — power,
  pitch, positive rate, flaps, then navigate the published missed approach; tell ATC.

### 11.5 The IFR flight, end to end (the Course 3 workflow)

1. Weather briefing, IFR go/no-go, alternate decision, fuel (L18.1).
2. File (or, in the sim, prepare the plan); build the G1000 flight plan (L18.4).
3. Get the clearance; copy and read back with CRAFT (L18.3).
4. Before-takeoff: set the departure, check the CDI source, brief the departure procedure.
5. Depart, climb, join the route; hand-fly or engage the autopilot (L18.5).
6. Brief the approach 10–15 minutes before descent (Appendix B.12).
7. Fly the approach; land or go missed; hold or divert (Module 20).
8. Log it: approaches, holds, instrument time (Section 18.4).

## 12. v2 regions: Bay Area extensions and the Sierra Nevada

> ⚠ **For every airport, navaid and procedure below:** verify runway designators, lengths,
> field elevation, pattern direction and altitude, frequencies, lighting, airspace and
> approach availability against (1) the current Chart Supplement, (2) the current d-TPP,
> (3) SkyVector and (4) the sim. Record the date in the region's `airports.yaml`. As in
> v1, frequencies are deliberately not listed in this plan.

### 12.1 How regions work

- A **region** is a named area with its own airports, navaids, landmarks, hazards, charts
  and airspace profile (for W11). Regions live in `content/regions/<slug>/`.
- v1's airports move into the `bay-area` region without changing their ICAO keys or URLs.
- Challenges have a `region`; the challenges list and airport cards filter by it.

| KMRY | Monterey Regional     | Towered, busy terrain | Flight following destination (C15.2); ILS/RNAV approaches; coastal fog |
| KSCK | Stockton Metropolitan | Class C, towered      | Class C operations; long runways for night and IFR practice            |
| KSAC | Sacramento Executive  | Class D, towered      | Gateway toward the Sierra; IFR alternate option                        |
| KOAK | Oakland International | Class C               | Now allowed for taxi only: hot-spot challenge (C10.5, P1)              |
| KSNS | Salinas Municipal     | Class D (v1 P1)       | IFR approaches; alternate for Monterey                                 |

| ---- | ----------------------------- | ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| KGOO | Nevada County (Grass Valley)  | Non-towered, foothills       | Alternate foothill departure; fire-fighting base (landmark)         |

| KMEV | Minden–Tahoe                  | Non-towered, ~4,700 ft       | Lee-side airport; mountain wave and glider activity                 |

- **Navaids (⚠ verify, MON programme):** SAC (Sacramento), SWR (Squaw Valley), FMG
  (Mustang), plus v1's ECA and OAK for the Central Valley legs.
- **Landmarks (⚠ verify in sim scenery):** Folsom Lake, the American River canyons, the
  I-80 corridor and Donner Summit, Donner Lake, Lake Tahoe, Emerald Bay, Carson Valley.
- **Hazards:** terrain above 9,000 ft; density altitude; afternoon convection in summer;
  mountain wave in westerly flow; smoke from wildfires reducing visibility; winter snow
  (challenges use late-summer dates).
- **Charts:** San Francisco sectional (west side) and **Klamath Falls / Las Vegas**
  sectionals for parts of the region (⚠ verify coverage), plus the IFR low chart.

Pick procedures that exist in **both** the current d-TPP and the sim's nav database.
Record the chosen procedure name and the d-TPP cycle in the challenge YAML.

- **Holding fixes (⚠ verify):** a published hold at the KLVK approach's missed-approach
  holding fix (C20.5); an ATC-style hold on a radial of ECA VOR clear of Class C airspace
  (C19.1); a GPS hold at an approach IAF/IF with a hold-in-lieu of a procedure turn, or a

- **Obstacle departure procedures:** check the KLVK takeoff minimums and ODP (C18.3).

### 12.5 `content/regions/<slug>/` files (see Section 27.2)

`region.yaml` (slug, name, summary, bounds, charts, hazards, landmarks), `airports.yaml`
(v1 fields plus `regionSlug`, `lighting` {pcl, papi, reil, approachLights}, `procedures[]`
{name, type, dtppCycleVerified}), `navaids.yaml`, `airspace-profile.yaml` (W11 data).

# Part III — Curriculum

## 13. Curriculum design principles (what changes in v2)

All v1 principles stay (v1 Section 12): building blocks, Learn → See → Try → Fly →
Reflect, observable objectives, cognitive load, mastery, professional habits, honesty
about the sim, accessible content and the three-tier tolerances. v2 adds the following.

### 13.1 Courses as building blocks

1. **Course 1 — Skyhawk Foundations** (v1, Modules 0–8): control the airplane, fly a
   pattern, navigate VFR in good weather.
2. **Course 2 — Skyhawk Advanced VFR** (Modules 9–16): performance, crosswind, weather,
   night, mountains, failures, busy airspace and online ATC.
3. **Course 3 — Skyhawk Instrument Foundations** (Modules 17–21): fly by instruments,
   understand the IFR system, hold, fly approaches.

Course 2 recommends Course 1; Course 3 recommends Course 2's Modules 11 and 14 (weather
and failures). Recommendations are soft warnings, never hard locks (v1 Section 13.3).
Riley (Section 3.3) can start Course 3 directly.

### 13.2 Fly the same skill in new conditions

v2 deliberately re-flies v1 skills in harder conditions — a pattern at night, a landing
at 6,000 ft, a cross-country with a failure — so learners see that the fundamentals don't
change, only the margins do. Every v2 challenge scores at least one v1 fundamental
(airspeed, altitude or a checklist).

### 13.3 Judgement is a skill (D2-08)

- A challenge can include **decision criteria**: "Decided to divert before the cloud base
  dropped below 1,000 ft AGL." Making the safe decision is a pass even if the flight never
  reaches its planned destination.
- Debriefs always ask "What was your decision point, and when did you reach it?"
- Lessons use the new **Decision** callout (Section 19.2) at the moment a real pilot would
  make a go/no-go call.

### 13.4 Surprise, not rehearsal

Failures, go-arounds and diversions arrive through **random events** at unpredictable
times (Section 16.1). A learner who knows the failure is coming at minute 3 is rehearsing,
not training. Variants (Section 16.1) change which failure or which weather appears.

### 13.5 Automation is a tool, not a crutch

Course 3 teaches every procedure **hand-flown first**, then with the autopilot. Challenge
pairs (C20.2 hand-flown ILS, C20.3 coupled ILS) make this explicit, and the Instrument
stage check requires both.

### 13.6 Retention needs practice

Memory items, limits, light signals, weather minimums, lost-comms rules and holding
entries are **drilled** in the practice hub (Section 18), not just read once. Each v2
lesson lists the flashcard deck it feeds.

### 13.7 IFR tolerances (the "Learn-To-Fly instrument standard")

Inspired by the Instrument Rating ACS. Visual tolerances (v1 Section 12.9) apply to VFR
challenges; IFR challenges use this table:

| Tier        | Meaning                                     | Altitude | Heading | Airspeed | Course (CDI)                            | DA / MDA                              |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------- | -------- | ------- | -------- | --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| ⭐ Bronze   | "Safe, kept it upright and legal"           | ±200 ft  | ±20°    | ±15 kt   | Never full-scale for more than a moment | Never below DA/MDA without the runway |
| ⭐⭐ Silver | "Good instrument student"                   | ±150 ft  | ±15°    | ±10 kt   | Within full scale                       | +150/−0 ft                            |
| ⭐⭐⭐ Gold | "Instrument-checkride-ready (ACS-inspired)" | ±100 ft  | ±10°    | ±10 kt   | Within ¾ scale                          | +100/−0 ft                            |

- **Descending below DA or MDA without the required visual references is a required
  criterion that cannot be met** — the attempt does not pass, whatever else happened.
- Holding tolerances: inbound leg 1 min ±15 s at Gold (±30 s Silver, ±45 s Bronze).

### 13.8 Night and mountain safety framing

Night, mountain and IFR lessons open with a short **"In a real airplane"** box explaining
the additional training a real pilot would need (e.g. a mountain flying course with an
instructor). This keeps v1 principle 12.7 (honesty about the sim) visible where it matters
most.

|        | **3 total**      |                                           | **19** | **1** | **16** | **4**  | **~23 h** |
|        | **v2 new total** |                                           | **52** | **4** | **42** | **12** | **~55 h** |

Course 1 counts include v1's 7 P1 lessons and 6 P1 challenges, which become P0 in
Phase 15 (D2-03).

### 14.3 Completion rules (changes to v1 Section 13.3)

- A **module** is complete when all its P0 lessons are complete and all its P0 challenges
  are passed (unchanged).
- A **course** is complete when all its modules are complete **and** its stage check
  (C8.1 for Course 1, C16.1 for Course 2, C21.1 for Course 3) is passed at **Silver or
  better**.
- Course badges: "Skyhawk Pilot (Sim)" (v1), "Skyhawk Advanced VFR (Sim)", "Skyhawk
  Instrument Foundations (Sim)". Each badge page repeats that it is not a real
  certificate.
- Existing v1 learners keep their v1 badge. If they completed Course 1 before the Silver
  rule existed, the badge is not revoked (migration 003, Section 36.3).

### 14.4 Slugs and IDs

- Course slugs: `skyhawk-foundations`, `skyhawk-advanced-vfr`,
  `skyhawk-instrument-foundations` (URL `/courses/skyhawk-advanced-vfr`). Course display
  codes are `Course 1`–`Course 3`; there is no `C1`-style code, so they never clash with
  challenge codes.
- Module slugs as listed in Section 14.2; lesson and challenge slugs follow v1
  Section 13.4 (e.g. `l12-3-night-operations-preflight-to-landing`,
  `c20-2-hand-flown-ils`).
- Region slugs: `bay-area`, `sierra-nevada`.
- Flashcard deck slugs: `memory-items`, `limits-and-speeds`, `light-signals`,
  `airspace-and-minimums`, `weather-codes`, `chart-symbols`, `radio-phraseology`,
  `ifr-rules`, `approach-plate-symbols`, `holding-entries`.

---

## 15. Lesson specifications (every v2 lesson)

Fields as v1 Section 14: **Est.** · **Prereqs** · **Widgets** · **Challenges**, then
**Objectives**, **Outline** (each item becomes an `##` section), **Key facts** (⚠ verify),
**Callouts**, **Knowledge check** (correct answer in bold), **Practice** (flashcard deck
fed by the lesson, new in v2) and **Go deeper** (resource slugs).

### Module 9 — Performance, weight and balance

#### L9.1 — Density altitude and performance (P0)

- **Est.** 20 min · **Prereqs** Course 1 · **Widgets** W21 · **Challenges** C9.1
- **Objectives**
  1. Calculate pressure altitude and density altitude from field elevation, altimeter
     setting and temperature.
  2. Explain how density altitude affects engine power, propeller thrust and lift.
  3. Predict whether a takeoff will be longer or shorter than on a standard day.
- **Outline**
  1. Why the same runway is "shorter" on a hot afternoon (story: KLVK in August).
  2. Pressure altitude: the altimeter-setting correction.
  3. Density altitude: temperature's effect, with W21.
  4. What changes: power, thrust, lift, true airspeed on final, groundspeed.
  5. Rules of thumb and when to stop trusting them.
  6. Density altitude in the sim: how temperature and pressure presets change it.
- **Key facts** (⚠ verify): PA = elevation + (29.92 − altimeter) × 1,000; ISA lapse 2 °C
  per 1,000 ft; DA ≈ PA + 120 × (OAT − ISA temp) (Appendix F.1).
- **Callouts**: Safety — "High, hot and heavy" accidents; Sim vs reality — sim engine
  power loss with altitude may differ; Decision — "Would you take off?"
- **Knowledge check**
  1. KLVK elevation ~400 ft, altimeter 29.82, OAT 38 °C. Density altitude? →
     **About 3,400 ft** (numeric, ±300 ft; ⚠ recompute with verified elevation).
  2. Which increases density altitude? → **Higher temperature** (single).
  3. On a high-DA day your indicated approach speed should be… → **the same; your
     groundspeed will be higher.**
- **Practice**: `limits-and-speeds` (DA facts).
- **Go deeper**: `phak-performance`, `faa-density-altitude-brochure`.

#### L9.2 — Reading performance charts (P0)

- **Est.** 25 min · **Prereqs** L9.1 · **Widgets** W22 · **Challenges** C9.1
- **Objectives**
  1. Read takeoff and landing distance from a table, interpolating between rows.
  2. Apply headwind, tailwind and grass corrections.
  3. Add a safety margin and compare with the runway available.
- **Outline**
  1. What a performance chart tells you (and what it assumes: technique, new engine,
     dry paved runway).
  2. Reading the Learn-To-Fly sim-data chart with W22 (D2-11).
  3. Interpolation, step by step.
  4. Corrections: wind, surface, slope.
  5. Margins: a common personal rule is +50% (⚠ phrase as a personal minimum, not a rule).
  6. Landing distance and why it matters more at short fields.
- **Callouts**: Note — "Always use the POH for a real airplane"; Sim vs reality — our table
  comes from sim tests; Pro tip — do the numbers before you need them.
- **Knowledge check**: two numeric interpolation questions from W22; one `single` on
  tailwind correction direction → **increases distance**.
- **Practice**: none. **Go deeper**: `phak-performance`, `afh-takeoffs`.

#### L9.3 — Weight and balance (P0)

- **Est.** 25 min · **Prereqs** L9.2 · **Widgets** W23 · **Challenges** C9.2
- **Objectives**
  1. Compute total weight, moment and CG for a given load.
  2. Decide whether a load is within the CG envelope and maximum weight.
  3. Explain how forward and aft CG change handling and stall behaviour.
- **Outline**
  1. Why weight and balance matters (stability, stall recovery, performance).
  2. Datum, arm, moment, CG — with W23.
  3. The loading table and the envelope (Section 8.2).
  4. Fuel burn moves the CG.
  5. Fixing an out-of-limits load: move bags, reduce fuel, leave a passenger.
  6. Setting payload and fuel in the sim so it matches your plan.
- **Key facts**: station arms and envelope (Section 8.2, ⚠ verify); 6 lb per gallon.
- **Callouts**: Safety — aft CG and stall recovery; Sim vs reality — how the sim's payload
  screen maps to our stations.
- **Knowledge check**: one numeric CG calculation (±0.3 in); `single` "Aft CG makes the
  airplane… → **less stable and harder to recover from a stall**"; `order` the steps to
  compute CG.
- **Practice**: `limits-and-speeds`. **Go deeper**: `faa-weight-balance-handbook`,
  `phak-weight-balance`.

#### L9.4 — Leaning the mixture and fuel management (P0)

- **Est.** 20 min · **Prereqs** L1.3 · **Widgets** W24 (P1) · **Challenges** C9.3
- **Objectives**
  1. Lean for cruise using EGT and fuel flow per the in-sim checklist.
  2. Compute endurance and reserve for a planned flight.
  3. Manage the fuel selector and recognise fuel imbalance.
- **Outline**
  1. Why lean: power, economy, spark-plug fouling.
  2. Leaning methods (peak EGT, rich of peak; ⚠ verify the method in the in-sim checklist).
  3. Leaning for climb and for high-altitude takeoffs (links forward to L13.3).
  4. Fuel planning: burn, reserve (30 min day, 45 min night — v1 Section 10.9), personal
     buffer.
  5. Fuel imbalance and the selector.
- **Callouts**: Sim vs reality — engine roughness modelling; Pro tip — note fuel at every
  checkpoint.
- **Knowledge check**: `single` "Leaning too far past peak causes… → **roughness and power
  loss**"; `numeric` endurance question.
- **Practice**: `limits-and-speeds`. **Go deeper**: `phak-systems`, `aopa-leaning`.

### Module 10 — Advanced takeoffs and landings

#### L10.1 — Crosswinds revisited: gusts, wind shear and limits (P0)

- **Est.** 20 min · **Prereqs** L4.5 · **Widgets** W13, W25 · **Challenges** C10.1
- **Objectives**
  1. Compute crosswind and headwind components, including gusts.
  2. Apply the gust-factor speed additive on final.
  3. Decide when a crosswind exceeds your personal limit and pick another runway or
     airport.
- **Outline**
  1. Recap: crab vs wing-low (W25).
  2. Gusts: add half the gust factor to the approach speed (⚠ verify the AFH wording).
  3. Wind shear near the ground: what it feels like and what to do (go around).
  4. Maximum demonstrated crosswind is not a limit — but it is a good personal one.
  5. Choosing the runway and the airport: using W13 with real wind.
  6. Controls on the rollout and taxi (aileron into the wind).
- **Callouts**: Safety — loss of control on landing; Decision — "Crosswind above your
  limit? Divert."; Sim vs reality — ground handling in gusts.
- **Knowledge check**: numeric gust additive (wind 12G20 → **+4 kt**); W13 target;
  `single` on rollout aileron position.
- **Practice**: none. **Go deeper**: `afh-landings`, `boldmethod-crosswind`.

#### L10.2 — Slips (P0)

- **Est.** 15 min · **Prereqs** L10.1 · **Widgets** W25 · **Challenges** C10.2
- **Objectives**
  1. Explain the difference between a forward slip and a side slip.
  2. Fly a forward slip to lose altitude without gaining airspeed.
  3. Recover from the slip before touchdown.
- **Outline**
  1. The two slips and what each is for (W25 side-by-side).
  2. Entering, holding and recovering from a forward slip.
  3. Slips with flaps (⚠ verify any POH caution for the 172S).
  4. The side slip as the wing-low crosswind technique.
  5. Airspeed indication in a slip.
- **Callouts**: Sim vs reality — rudder authority with a twist stick or gamepad (auto-rudder
  users can't slip properly; the lesson says so and offers a P1 variant).
- **Knowledge check**: `single` "In a forward slip the nose points… → **away from the
  lowered wing, off the ground track**"; `order` slip recovery steps.
- **Go deeper**: `afh-landings`.

#### L10.3 — Soft-field takeoffs and landings (P0)

- **Est.** 15 min · **Prereqs** L4.1, L4.3 · **Challenges** C10.3
- **Objectives**
  1. Fly a soft-field takeoff using ground effect.
  2. Fly a soft-field landing with the nose wheel held off.
  3. Taxi on a soft surface without stopping.
- **Outline**
  1. What makes a field "soft" and why it matters.
  2. Taxi: keep moving, elevator back.
  3. Takeoff: flaps (⚠ verify setting), lift off early, accelerate in ground effect.
  4. Landing: touch down at the slowest possible speed, keep the nose up, some power.
  5. Practising on a paved runway (simulated soft field) and on a real grass strip.
- **Callouts**: Sim vs reality — how the sim models surfaces (Week 2 notes).
- **Knowledge check**: `single` "Why stay in ground effect after lift-off? → **To
  accelerate with less induced drag**"; `multi` soft-field landing actions.
- **Go deeper**: `afh-takeoffs`, `afh-landings`.

#### L10.4 — Short-field mastery and obstacles (P0)

- **Est.** 20 min · **Prereqs** L4.6, L9.2 · **Widgets** W22 · **Challenges** C10.4
- **Objectives**
  1. Plan a short-field takeoff over a 50-ft obstacle with W22.
  2. Fly a short-field approach at the short-field speed and touch down at the aiming
     point.
  3. Explain the trade-off between Vx and Vy.
- **Outline**
  1. Recap of v1 L4.6.
  2. Obstacle clearance: distance to 50 ft, Vx, and when to switch to Vy.
  3. The precise approach: 61 KIAS (⚠ verify), stabilised early, power to control the
     glide path.
  4. Maximum braking without skidding; flaps up for braking effectiveness (⚠ verify).
- **Callouts**: Decision — "Numbers don't fit? Don't go." Safety — obstacle chart
  symbols.
- **Knowledge check**: numeric W22 question; `single` Vx vs Vy definition.
- **Go deeper**: `afh-takeoffs`, `afh-landings`.

#### L10.5 — Runway safety: wake turbulence and hot spots (P0)

- **Est.** 15 min · **Prereqs** L3.2 · **Widgets** W8 · **Challenges** C10.5 (P1)
- **Objectives**
  1. Describe how wingtip vortices behave and how to avoid them on takeoff and landing.
  2. Identify a hot spot on an airport diagram and brief it before taxi.
  3. Read back every hold-short instruction.
- **Outline**
  1. Wingtip vortices: sink, drift, linger (diagram).
  2. Avoidance: land beyond, take off before, wait (AIM, ⚠ verify spacing guidance).
  3. Runway incursions: the hold-short line, the runway holding position sign (W8 recap).
  4. Hot spots on airport diagrams and in the Chart Supplement.
  5. The taxi brief: route, hot spots, hold-short points.
- **Knowledge check**: `single` "Landing behind a larger airplane, touch down… →
  **beyond its touchdown point**"; hotspot question on W8.
- **Practice**: `radio-phraseology` (hold-short readbacks). **Go deeper**:
  `aim-wake-turbulence`, `faa-runway-safety`.

#### L10.6 — Power-off accuracy landings (P1)

- **Est.** 15 min · **Prereqs** L10.2, L5.4 · **Widgets** W15 · **Challenges** C10.6
- **Objectives**: Fly a power-off 180 from abeam the touchdown point to a landing within
  200 ft beyond it (commercial ACS-inspired).
- **Outline**: why it's the best engine-failure practice; the key position; energy
  management with flaps and slips; judging the base turn.
- **Go deeper**: `afh-emergency`, `commercial-acs`.

### Module 11 — Weather for pilots

#### L11.1 — Weather theory: air masses, fronts and stability (P0)

- **Est.** 25 min · **Prereqs** L6.7 · **Widgets** W26 (P1)
- **Objectives**
  1. Describe the weather typically found with cold and warm fronts.
  2. Predict cloud type and visibility from stability.
  3. Explain the Bay Area's sea breeze and marine layer.
- **Outline**: the atmosphere in 5 minutes; air masses; fronts (W26 cross-section);
  stability and lapse rates; moisture and the temperature/dewpoint spread; local weather:
  sea breeze, marine layer, valley heating.
- **Knowledge check**: `single` "Stable air brings… → **stratus clouds and poor
  visibility**"; `multi` cold-front weather.
- **Practice**: `weather-codes`. **Go deeper**: `aviation-weather-handbook`,
  `phak-weather-theory`.

#### L11.2 — Forecasts and briefings (P0)

- **Est.** 30 min · **Prereqs** L11.1 · **Widgets** W17 · **Challenges** C11.1
- **Objectives**
  1. Decode a TAF, including FM, TEMPO and PROB groups.
  2. Read the GFA, winds aloft, AIRMETs, SIGMETs and PIREPs for a route.
  3. Organise a standard briefing in a logical order.
- **Outline**: the standard briefing order (adverse conditions first); TAFs with W17 in
  TAF mode; GFA walkthrough (annotated screenshots); winds and temperatures aloft;
  AIRMETs, SIGMETs, PIREPs (tables from Section 10.2); a worked briefing for KLVK → KMRY.
- **Callouts**: Sim vs reality — sim weather presets are not forecasts; Decision — "What's
  the worst thing on this briefing?"
- **Knowledge check**: TAF decode (`single`, ×2); `order` briefing items.
- **Practice**: `weather-codes`. **Go deeper**: `awc-gfa`, `1800wxbrief`.

#### L11.3 — Clouds, fog and low visibility (P0)

- **Est.** 20 min · **Prereqs** L11.1 · **Widgets** W17 · **Challenges** C11.2
- **Objectives**: identify fog types and when they form; explain why ceiling and
  visibility can drop faster than forecast; state VFR minimums for the airspace you plan
  to use (v1 Section 10.2).
- **Outline**: radiation, advection, upslope fog; the marine layer at KHAF and KMRY;
  scud and "just one more mile"; ceilings in METARs; visibility and haze from smoke.
- **Callouts**: Safety — scud running; Decision — the turn-back point.
- **Knowledge check**: `single` fog type from a scenario → **advection fog**; `numeric`
  temp/dewpoint spread.
- **Go deeper**: `aviation-weather-handbook`.

#### L11.4 — Thunderstorms, icing and turbulence (P0)

- **Est.** 20 min · **Prereqs** L11.1
- **Objectives**: state the three ingredients of a thunderstorm and the avoidance
  distance; explain why the Skyhawk must avoid icing; recognise turbulence forecasts.
- **Outline**: thunderstorm life cycle; avoid by 20 nm (⚠ verify AIM wording); icing
  types and why the C172 is not certified for known icing (⚠ verify for the 172S);
  turbulence types, Va and slowing down (Section 8.1).
- **Callouts**: Safety — no aircraft in this course flies near thunderstorms or icing.
- **Knowledge check**: `multi` thunderstorm ingredients; `single` "In turbulence, fly at
  or below… → **Va for your weight**".
- **Go deeper**: `aviation-weather-handbook`, `aim-ch7`.

#### L11.5 — Weather in MSFS 2024 (P0)

- **Est.** 15 min · **Prereqs** L0.2 · **Challenges** C11.4 (P1)
- **Objectives**: set up custom weather to match a scenario; explain the limits of live
  weather in the sim; reproduce a challenge's weather preset exactly.
- **Outline**: presets vs custom vs live (Section 9.3); cloud layers, visibility, gusts;
  how to recreate a real METAR in the sim; known differences.
- **Callouts**: Sim vs reality — at length.
- **Go deeper**: `msfs-forums`, `msfs-official-site`.

#### L11.6 — The go/no-go decision and VFR into IMC (P0)

- **Est.** 25 min · **Prereqs** L11.2, L11.3 · **Challenges** C11.1, C11.3
- **Objectives**
  1. Apply PAVE and personal minimums to a real briefing.
  2. Set a turn-back point before departure.
  3. Fly a 180° turn on instruments after inadvertent cloud entry.
- **Outline**: PAVE and IMSAFE recap (v1 L8.1); writing personal minimums (worksheet
  saved to the profile, P1); plan continuation bias; the 180° turn (Section 10.3); getting
  help (ATC, 121.5, squawk 7700).
- **Callouts**: Decision — the whole lesson; Safety — VFR into IMC statistics
  (⚠ cite source).
- **Knowledge check**: scenario `single` questions; `order` the 180° turn steps.
- **Practice**: `airspace-and-minimums`, `memory-items` (VFR into IMC).
- **Go deeper**: `risk-management-handbook`, `aopa-vfr-into-imc`.

### Module 12 — Night flying

#### L12.1 — Night vision and illusions (P0)

- **Est.** 15 min · **Prereqs** Course 1 · **Widgets** W28 (P1)
- **Objectives**: explain dark adaptation and off-centre viewing; name three night
  illusions and how to counter them.
- **Outline**: rods and cones; dark adaptation; off-centre scanning; black-hole approach,
  autokinesis, false horizon (W28); what the sim can and can't show.
