# Part VII — Quality, risk, legal and launch

## 51. In-sim verification additions

The v1 protocol (v1 Section 54) applies to every v2 item. v2 adds:

### 51.1 Additional sources of truth

Insert into v1's priority list (v1 Section 54.1), after the Chart Supplement:

- **Current d-TPP and IFR enroute charts** — for procedures, minimums, holds and ODPs.
- **The sim's nav database** — for what the learner can actually load. When the sim and
  the d-TPP differ, teach the d-TPP version and fly the sim's, with a "Sim vs reality"
  callout (as v1 does for airport data).

### 51.2 Verification record additions

```yaml
lastVerifiedAt: 2027-05-01
simVersion: 'MSFS 2024 SU x (1.x.x.x)'
navDataCycle: 'sim default 24xx' # or 'Navigraph 27xx'
dtppCycle: '2705' # IFR only
verifiedAgainst:
  - 'In-sim C172 G1000 checklist'
  - 'FAA d-TPP cycle 2705: ILS or LOC RWY 25R, KLVK'
  - 'Chart Supplement SW, effective …'
```

### 51.3 Challenge verification additions

1. Build every variant from the brief only; every setup override must be possible.
2. Trigger every event with the test-only short timer, then once with the real timer.
3. For auto criteria, work the planning answers by hand and with the widget; both must
   agree within Gold tolerance.
4. For decision challenges, fly the "safe" and the "unsafe" decision; the rubric must
   pass the first and fail the second.
5. For IFR challenges, fly hand-flown and coupled; check the DA/MDA criterion fails if you
   descend below minimums.
6. Record the verification data (Section 51.2).

### 51.4 After sim updates and chart cycles (post-release routine)

- **Every 28 days (d-TPP cycle):** run `npm run content:cycles`; re-check any procedure
  whose plate changed (the FAA publishes change notices; ⚠ find the current way to see
  "changed" plates); update `dtppCycle`.
- **Every sim update:** v1 Section 54.5 routine, plus re-fly C12.1 (night lighting), C14.1
  (electrical), C20.2 (ILS) and one variant of C10.1 (crosswind).
- The admin verification dashboard (P1) lists items that are overdue.

---

## 52. Release QA checklist (v2)

Run against production (or a production-like preview) before tagging `v2.0.0`. The v1
checklist (v1 Section 55) is run again in full first.

### 52.1 Functional

- [ ] Course catalog and overview pages; `/learn` course tabs; v1 URLs unchanged.
- [ ] Every P0 v2 lesson opens without errors (click through all 52).
- [ ] Every P0 v2 challenge opens; variants roll and display; kneeboard prints.
- [ ] Planning tab: "Check my numbers" matches the server result after submit.
- [ ] Events fire in C11.3, C14.1–C14.5, C17.3, C17.4, C19.1, C20.6 (P1), C21.1 with sound
      and card; timestamps saved.
- [ ] Legs work in C12.3, C13.2, C16.1, C16.2, C21.1, C21.2.
- [ ] Decision criteria and auto criteria display correctly in debrief and result.
- [ ] Stage checks: gate warning; course completion at Silver; badge awarded; v1 badge
      holders kept theirs.
- [ ] Logbook: auto entry from a flight challenge; manual entry; edit; delete (manual
      only); totals; CSV export opens correctly in a spreadsheet.
- [ ] Currency panel: day, night and instrument states with seeded test data.
- [ ] Practice: due count, session, ratings, next due date, decks add/remove; visitor
      practice without saving.
- [ ] Password reset end to end with a real email in production; old sessions revoked.
- [ ] Classic-panel mode swaps callouts and images.
- [ ] Tools hub: every tool works and prints.
- [ ] Reference: regions, airports (lighting, procedures), weather, plates, emergencies.
- [ ] Account export contains logbook, practice and planning data; deletion removes them.
- [ ] Dashboard v2 sections correct for a new user, a v1 graduate and a Course 3 learner.

### 52.2 Non-functional

- [ ] v1 non-functional checks (v1 Section 55.2) pass again.
- [ ] Section 4.2 v2 targets met (INP, logbook and practice latency).
- [ ] axe clean on new pages and dialogs; keyboard-only runs of Flows E–I.
- [ ] Email: SPF/DKIM pass (check headers of a received email); no tokens in logs.
- [ ] CSV injection test (entry remarks starting with `=`).
- [ ] Migrations recorded in `migrations`; counts before/after documented in the PR.
- [ ] Backups: Atlas snapshot or `mongodump` from the day of release.

### 52.3 Content

- [ ] `content:validate --strict` passes; `content:links` 0 broken; `content:cycles` shows
      no procedure older than two cycles.
- [ ] Zero "TODO", "TBD", "lorem" (grep).
- [ ] Every IFR page shows the IFR disclaimer; every real procedure shows its cycle and
      "Not for navigation".
- [ ] Every performance number shows "Learn-To-Fly sim data" labelling.
- [ ] Glossary ≥ 300 terms; flashcards ≥ 250.

### 52.4 Legal

- [ ] Privacy policy updated (email provider, logbook, practice, time zone, weather P1).
- [ ] Terms updated (IFR disclaimer, logbook not a legal record).
- [ ] About page trademark list updated (Section 54.3).
- [ ] Training plate watermark present; no real plate crops embedded.

---

## 53. Risks and mitigations

v1 risks R1–R16 (v1 Section 56) still apply. v2-specific risks:

| #     | Risk                                                               | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation                                                                                                                        |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------- | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R2-01 | **Scope explosion** — IFR and new features grow without end        | High       | High   | "Foundations, not a rating" (D2-06); guardrails (Section 2.4); P1 cuts first; Course 3 can ship as `v2.1` if M2-E slips > 6 weeks |
| R2-02 | **IFR content used for real flight**                               | Medium     | High   | IFR disclaimers on every IFR page and footer; cycles and "Not for navigation"; training plate is fictional (D2-10)                |
| R2-03 | **Sim features differ or change** (failures, PCL, weather, ATC)    | High       | Medium | Instructor-style failures (D2-09); intent-based instructions; verification after every sim update (Section 51.4)                  |
| R2-04 | **Nav data mismatch** between sim and current charts               | High       | Medium | Choose procedures present in both; record both cycles; `content:cycles`; teach current, fly sim's                                 |
| R2-05 | **Content volume** (~110 items + cards) overwhelms the author      | High       | High   | Draft-then-verify model (v1 D-21); publish module by module (D2-17); cut P1 content first; realistic schedule (Section 37)        |
| R2-06 | **Author's IFR, night and mountain knowledge gaps**                | Medium     | High   | 11-week study path (Section 7); IFH/IPH as primary sources; recruit an instrument-rated reviewer (Phase 25)                       |
| R2-07 | **Migration damages v1 user data**                                 | Low        | High   | Additive, idempotent migrations; fixture tests; dry run; backups; runbook (Section 36.4)                                          |
| R2-08 | **Password reset abuse / email deliverability**                    | Medium     | Medium | Rate limits; no enumeration; hashed single-use tokens; SPF/DKIM/DMARC; provider dashboards                                        |
| R2-09 | **Self-assessment feels even less credible for IFR precision**     | Medium     | Medium | Auto-graded planning and drills; decision criteria; replay advice; `telemetry` specs ready for the companion (D2-04)              |
| R2-10 | **Widget load** (13 new + 7 carry-over) blows the schedule         | Medium     | Medium | Timeboxes (Section 17.21); simplest first; static annotated images as a fallback for P0 lessons                                   |
| R2-11 | **VATSIM etiquette issues** from learners sent by us               | Low        | Medium | Code of Conduct emphasis, "listen first", encourage VATSIM's own training; the challenge is P1 and optional                       |
| R2-12 | **Mountain and IFR challenges too hard on a gamepad**              | Medium     | Low    | Calm variants; auto-rudder notes; honest difficulty dots; P1 anywhere mode for practice                                           |
| R2-13 | **Performance data accuracy/copyright**                            | Low        | Medium | Own sim-test tables (D2-11); "sim data" labels; POH reminder                                                                      |
| R2-14 | **Atlas storage/connection limits** with logbook and practice data | Low        | Medium | Indexes; totals cache; move to M10 (Section 35.3); monitor size monthly                                                           |
| R2-15 | **Burnout** over a ~9-month v2                                     | Medium     | High   | Milestones every 2–3 weeks (Section 37.3); ship `v1.1.0` and Course 2 early; celebrate each; it's OK to move P1s to v2.x          |
| R2-16 | **Live weather API changes or rate limits** (P1)                   | Medium     | Low    | Feature flag; cache; graceful fallback; never block a page on it                                                                  |

---

## 54. Legal additions

> Planning guidance, not legal advice (as v1 Section 57).

### 54.1 IFR and advanced-content disclaimer

Short version (IFR page footers, plates page, Course 3 overview):

> "Instrument procedures change every 28 days. Learn-To-Fly's instrument content is for
> simulation only and must never be used for real instrument flight, navigation or
> training."

Add to `/disclaimer`: night, mountain and instrument flying need specific real-world
training; the logbook and currency panel are habit trackers, not legal records; sim time
does not count toward any certificate, rating or currency (MSFS is not an approved
training device).

### 54.2 Online networks

- VATSIM and PilotEdge are independent organisations. Link to them; don't use their logos;
  state "not affiliated". Follow VATSIM's Code of Conduct in our own guidance and never
  suggest behaviour that breaks it. Check whether VATSIM has any guidance on third-party
  training material before publishing L15.5 (⚠).
- Pilot client screenshots: check the client's terms before publishing.

### 54.3 Trademarks (additions to v1 Section 57.3)

Add to the About page list: "VATSIM" (VATSIM Inc.), "PilotEdge", "Navigraph", "GFC 700"
(Garmin), "Resend"/"Postmark" only if named in the UI (normally only in the privacy
policy).

### 54.4 Charts and data

- FAA plates and charts: public domain, but embedded crops are avoided (D2-10); links
  point to the current FAA download pages; every mention carries "Not for navigation".
- aviationweather.gov data (P1): US Government data; follow the API's usage policy and
  attribution guidance (⚠ verify); label "real-world weather, not for real flight".

### 54.5 Email

- Transactional emails include the sender identity and a support contact; no marketing.
- Store only what's needed (email address already stored); the provider is a processor
  listed in the privacy policy.

## 55. The v2 demo script

A 15-minute demo. Rehearse twice.

### 55.1 Setup before the demo

- [ ] Production site open; demo account with Course 1 complete, Course 2 half done, a few
      Course 3 attempts, logbook entries and cards due.
- [ ] MSFS 2024 with the C172 G1000 at KLVK set up for C20.2 (`AIR_START_IMC` on the
      intercept heading, `WX_IMC_LOW`), paused.
- [ ] A phone with fly mode open; a printed kneeboard for C20.2.

### 55.2 Script

1. **(1 min) What changed.** "v1 taught you to fly the Skyhawk in good weather. v2 makes
   you a real all-round pilot in the same airplane."
2. **(1 min) Courses.** `/courses`: three courses, badges, stage checks.
3. **(2 min) Planning that grades itself.** C9.2: roll a scenario (aft CG), W23, "Check my
   numbers", fix the load.
4. **(1 min) Surprise failure.** C14.1 Fly tab with the demo short timer: the alternator
   failure card fires.
5. **(1 min) Practice.** A 6-card session: memory items and a W37 holding-entry card.
6. **(1 min) Logbook and currency.** "Night current until…", instrument currency needs
   two more approaches.
7. **(2 min) The approach plate explorer.** W38 brief mode, then W36: "the needle gets
   more sensitive as you get closer".
8. **(4 min) Fly it.** Switch to MSFS: fly the last few miles of the KLVK ILS to minimums
   and break out (or the pre-recorded clip).
9. **(1 min) Debrief.** Decision criterion "at DA with the runway in sight"; logbook
   section shows "1 ILS approach"; submit; currency updates.
10. **(1 min) What's next.** v3: the aircraft ladder; the SimConnect companion; more
    regions.

### 55.3 Backup plan

As v1 Section 58.3, plus: a recorded ILS clip, and a local build with the demo account
seeded.

## 56. Post-v2 roadmap

### 56.1 v2.x (1–3 months after release)

- Remaining P1 content, widgets and features (Section 2.2).
- Fixes and improvements from beta and release feedback.
- A second training plate (non-precision) for W38.
- More weather scenarios and flashcards.

### 56.2 The SimConnect companion (independent track, D2-04)

v1 Section 59.2 still describes it. v2 makes it more valuable: `telemetry` specs on v2
criteria, the logbook (auto-filled flight times, landings, approaches), and the currency
panel all become automatic when a companion reports the flight.

### 56.3 v3 — The aircraft ladder begins

The ladder from v1 Section 59.4 starts at step 2 (a high-performance/complex single),
reusing courses, regions, the challenge engine, the logbook and the practice hub. A v3 plan
(`v3.md`) is written at the end of Phase 26. Candidate first additions (⚠ verify what
MSFS 2024 includes and how well it is documented):

- **Course 4 — Complex single:** constant-speed propeller, retractable gear, higher
  speeds, the "complex" checkride.
- Then the ladder continues: light twin → turboprop → light jet → narrow-body → wide-body
  → **Airbus A380**.

### 56.4 v2 ideas deferred

- Full instrument rating syllabus (DME arcs, NDB, SIDs/STARs, partial-panel approaches in
  depth, classic-panel IFR).
- Commercial manoeuvres (chandelles, lazy eights, eights on pylons).
- UK/Europe region with hPa and metres; localisation.
- Speech recognition for readbacks.
- Instructor/classroom mode (from v1's parking lot).

### 56.5 Parking lot

Add freely; review at each milestone.

- Shared "community challenge of the week" (would need moderation — keep off).
- Logbook import from other sim logbook tools (Little Navmap logbook export?).
- Personal minimums worksheet saved to the profile and shown in go/no-go challenges.
- Integration with PilotEdge or VATSIM training records (read-only, if they offer APIs).
- Seasonal variants (winter Sierra with snow; icing awareness only).

# Appendices

## Appendix A — Glossary additions

Seed additions for `content/glossary.yaml` (≈ 150 new terms; the target after v2 is
≥ 300). Short, beginner-friendly definitions in our own words, written during Phases 22–23.
The list below is the minimum.

**Performance and W&B:** arm, basic empty weight, centre of gravity (CG), CG envelope,
datum, density altitude, ground effect, ISA, moment, pressure altitude, useful load, Vx,
Vy, Koch chart.

**Takeoffs and landings:** crab, forward slip, side slip, gust factor, maximum demonstrated
crosswind, soft field, short field, wind shear, wake turbulence, hot spot, runway
incursion, LAHSO (awareness), power-off 180.

**Weather:** air mass, advection fog, radiation fog, marine layer, stability, lapse rate,
front (cold, warm, stationary, occluded), TAF, FM/TEMPO/PROB, GFA, AIRMET (Sierra, Tango,
Zulu), SIGMET, convective SIGMET, PIREP, winds aloft (FB), ceiling, IMC, VMC, VFR into
IMC, thunderstorm, icing, turbulence, lenticular cloud.

**Night:** civil twilight, dark adaptation, off-centre viewing, black-hole approach,
autokinesis, false horizon, pilot-controlled lighting (PCL), REIL, approach lighting
system (ALS), VASI, PAPI, rotating beacon, position lights.

**Mountain:** mountain wave, rotor, lee side, windward side, ridge crossing, escape route,
canyon turn, density altitude (cross-ref).

**Systems and emergencies:** alternator, bus, load shedding, LOW VOLTS, pitot tube, static
port, alternate static source, AHRS, ADC, red X, reversionary mode, standby instruments,
emergency descent, fuel starvation, fuel exhaustion, Mayday, Pan-Pan, 7700, 7600.

**Airspace and ATC:** Class B clearance, Mode C veil, VFR flyway, VFR transition route,
flight following, traffic advisory, handoff, light gun signals, special VFR, readback,
VATSIM, UNICOM.

**IFR:** attitude instrument flying, control and performance, primary and supporting,
scan, fixation, partial panel, compass turn, ANDS, UNOS, spatial disorientation, the
leans, graveyard spiral, somatogravic illusion, IFR clearance, CRAFT, clearance limit,
void time, ODP, SID, STAR, airway, MEA, MOCA, MRA, IAF, IF, FAF, MAP, DA, MDA, VDP,
CDFA, step-down fix, LPV, LNAV, LNAV/VNAV, WAAS, RAIM, localizer, glideslope, ILS,
decision altitude, missed approach, holding pattern, direct/teardrop/parallel entry, EFC,
hold-in-lieu of procedure turn, procedure turn, circling approach, alternate, 1-2-3 rule,
lost communications, AVEF, d-TPP, AIRAC cycle, GFC 700, APR mode, FLC, VNV, GA mode.

## Appendix B — Advanced procedures summary (in our own words)

> ⚠ Summaries for learning in the sim only. Verify every item against the MSFS 2024
> in-sim checklist, the Cessna 172S POH and the FAA handbooks, and label them
> "Simplified for the sim" in the app (as v1 Appendix B).

### B.1 High density altitude takeoff

1. Compute DA and takeoff distance (W21, W22); set an abort point.
2. Run-up: lean for best power per the checklist method (⚠ verify).
3. Full power, check RPM and fuel flow; abort if the abort criteria aren't met.
4. Accelerate; lift off at the normal speed; climb at Vy; avoid turning toward rising
   terrain.

### B.2 Night preflight and departure

1. Flashlight (and a spare) within reach; panel and flood lights set; position lights on.
2. Beacon on before start; taxi light for taxi; strobes and landing light on entering the
   runway.
3. On climb-out: trust the attitude indicator and VSI until well clear of the ground.

### B.3 Ridge crossing

1. Plan the crossing altitude (at least 1,000–2,000 ft above the ridge) and the escape
   route before reaching the ridge.
2. Reach the crossing altitude early (circle over the valley if needed).
3. Approach the ridge at about 45°; if sink or turbulence is stronger than expected,
   turn away toward lower terrain.

### B.4 Alternator failure (flow)

1. Recognise: ammeter/load shows discharge, LOW VOLTS annunciation, voltage falling.
2. Check the ALT circuit breaker and switch (⚠ verify the checklist's reset guidance).
3. If not restored: ALT off; shed load (non-essential avionics, lights not needed).
4. Tell ATC; land as soon as practical; note battery time.

### B.5 Engine roughness / partial power loss (flow)

1. Fly the airplane: best glide or the best speed for the power available.
2. Fuel selector BOTH, fuel pump ON (⚠ verify), mixture as required, magnetos check
   (L, R, BOTH).
3. If power remains partial: land at the nearest suitable airport; if power is lost,
   forced landing (v1 Appendix B.16).

### B.6 Engine fire in flight (memory items, simplified)

1. Mixture idle cut-off; fuel selector OFF; auxiliary fuel pump OFF.
2. Master switch OFF; cabin heat and air OFF (except as needed); vents as required.
3. Airspeed as the checklist specifies (⚠ verify); forced landing (B.16 in v1).

### B.7 Emergency descent

Power idle, carb heat n/a (fuel-injected), mixture as required, bank 30–45°, airspeed
within limits (⚠ verify POH guidance and flap use), clear the area first.

### B.8 PFD failure

1. Press DISPLAY BACKUP (or confirm automatic reversion).
2. Check the reversionary display: attitude, airspeed, altitude, HSI, engine data.
3. Continue or divert as needed; brief the remaining capability aloud.

### B.9 Light gun signals (AIM)

| Signal                | On the ground                       | In the air                                    |
| --------------------- | ----------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
| Steady green          | Cleared for takeoff                 | Cleared to land                               |
| Flashing green        | Cleared to taxi                     | Return for landing (followed by steady green) |
| Steady red            | Stop                                | Give way to other aircraft; continue circling |
| Flashing red          | Taxi clear of the runway in use     | Airport unsafe — do not land                  |
| Flashing white        | Return to starting point on airport | Not applicable                                |
| Alternating red/green | Exercise extreme caution            | Exercise extreme caution                      |

### B.10 Unusual attitude recovery

- **Nose high:** add power, lower the nose to the horizon, level the wings, return to the
  desired attitude.
- **Nose low:** reduce power, level the wings, gently raise the nose to the horizon,
  return to the desired attitude.
- On partial panel, use the airspeed trend and altimeter for pitch and the turn indicator
  for bank.

### B.11 Holding: the 5 Ts at the fix

**Turn** to the outbound heading · **Time** (start the timer abeam the fix outbound) ·
**Twist** the course/OBS if needed · **Throttle** (holding speed) · **Talk** (report if
required).

### B.12 Approach briefing (kneeboard card fields)

Procedure name and date/cycle · frequencies (ATIS, approach, tower/CTAF, LOC/VOR with
identifier) · final approach course · FAF name and altitude · DA or MDA and required
visibility · touchdown zone/airport elevation · missed approach instructions (first
heading/altitude, holding fix) · notes (lighting, step-downs, VDP) · planned landing
runway and taxi plan.

### B.13 Missed approach flow

1. **Power:** full.
2. **Pitch:** climb attitude.
3. **Positive rate:** confirm on the VSI and altimeter.
4. **Flaps:** retract in stages per the checklist (⚠ verify).
5. **Navigate:** fly the published missed approach (check the CDI source and sequencing).
6. **Talk:** tell ATC ("missed approach").

### B.14 IFR lost communications (91.185, simplified)

- **Squawk 7600.** If in VMC, remain VMC and land as soon as practicable.
- **Route:** Assigned → Vectored (to the fix or route) → Expected → Filed.
- **Altitude:** the highest of assigned, MEA and the altitude you were told to expect, for
  each segment.
- Leave the clearance limit to start the approach at the expected time (or the ETA)
  (⚠ verify against the current 91.185 wording).

planningFields:

  - { id: cg, label: Centre of gravity, type: number, units: in, widget: weight-balance }

    label: Within limits?

variants:

      cg: { answer: 43.1, tolerance: { gold: 0.2, silver: 0.5, bronze: 1.0 } }

  - id: b
    label: Heavy bags in the back

      cg: { answer: 47.6, tolerance: { gold: 0.2, silver: 0.5, bronze: 1.0 } }

criteria:

    required: false
    weight: 1
  - id: pattern
    label: Pattern altitude
    kind: tiered
    tiers: { gold: '±100 ft', silver: '±150 ft', bronze: '±200 ft' }
    required: false

events: []

  holds: false

  - Forgetting that fuel burn moves the CG.

tips:
  - Enter the load in W23 first, then copy the numbers into the sim's payload screen.

debriefQuestions:
  - id: feel
    prompt: How did the airplane feel compared with flying solo?

## Appendix F — Aviation formulas and rules of thumb (v2 additions)

Implement in `shared/aviation/` with unit tests (v1 Appendix G conventions).

### F.1 Pressure and density altitude

```
PA (ft)          = field elevation + (29.92 − altimeter inHg) × 1,000
ISA temp (°C)    = 15 − 2 × (PA ÷ 1,000)
DA (ft)          ≈ PA + 120 × (OAT − ISA temp)
```

Example: elevation 400 ft, altimeter 29.82, OAT 38 °C → PA 500 ft, ISA 14 °C,
DA ≈ 500 + 120 × 24 ≈ 3,380 ft.

### F.2 Bilinear interpolation (performance tables)

```
given x between x0 and x1, y between y0 and y1, and table values f(x0,y0), f(x1,y0),
f(x0,y1), f(x1,y1):
tx = (x − x0) ÷ (x1 − x0);  ty = (y − y0) ÷ (y1 − y0)
f  = f00 × (1−tx)(1−ty) + f10 × tx(1−ty) + f01 × (1−tx)ty + f11 × tx·ty
```

Outside the table → no answer ("outside the chart — don't extrapolate").

### F.3 Weight and balance

```
moment        = weight × arm              (lb-in; often shown ÷ 1,000)
total weight  = Σ weights
CG (in)       = Σ moments ÷ total weight
fuel weight   = gallons × 6 lb            (100LL)
weight shift: Δweight ÷ total weight = ΔCG ÷ distance moved
```

### F.4 Descent planning and VDP

```
3° glidepath: height (ft) ≈ distance (nm) × 318
descent rate (fpm) ≈ GS (kt) × 5          (v1 G.8)
VDP distance from threshold (nm) ≈ HAT (ft) ÷ 300
CDFA descent rate = (FAF altitude − MDA) ÷ time from FAF to the point where the
                    descent should reach MDA
```

### F.5 Holding entry sectors (for W37)

With `C` = inbound course to the fix and `H` = aircraft heading when crossing the fix,
`rel = normalise(H − C)` to −180…180. For **right** (standard) turns:

```
direct    if −70 ≤ rel ≤ 110          (180° sector)
teardrop  if 110 < rel ≤ 180          (70° sector)
parallel  if −180 < rel < −70         (110° sector)
```

For **left** turns use `rel' = −rel` in the same rules. At an exact boundary either
adjacent entry is accepted (as in practice).
⚠ Verify against the AIM holding-entry figure; unit-test every boundary.

Timing: inbound leg 1 min (≤ 14,000 ft MSL). Outbound correction:
`new outbound time = old outbound time − (inbound time − 60 s)` (first approximation).
Wind correction outbound ≈ 3 × inbound WCA.

### F.6 CDI and ILS geometry (for W36)

```
angular deflection (dots/scale) = angle off course ÷ full-scale angle
localizer full scale   ≈ ±2.5° typical (tailored so the course is ~700 ft wide at the
                          threshold; ⚠ verify)
glideslope full scale  ≈ ±0.7°
linear GPS full scale  = ENR ±2.0 nm, TERM ±1.0 nm, APPR (LNAV) ±0.3 nm at the FAF
                          (⚠ verify G1000 NXi values)
lateral offset (ft)    ≈ distance (ft) × tan(angle)
```

Example: 1° off the localizer at 5 nm ≈ 30,380 ft × 0.0175 ≈ 530 ft; at 1 nm ≈ 106 ft.

### F.7 Magnetic compass (for W35)

- **ANDS** (northern hemisphere, on east/west headings): accelerate → compass shows a
  turn to the north; decelerate → to the south.
- **UNOS** turning errors: when turning to north, roll out **before** the heading
  (undershoot); to south, roll out **after** it (overshoot). Lead/lag ≈ latitude
  (+ half the bank angle in some teaching versions — ⚠ pick one and state it).
- Bay Area latitude ≈ 37–38° N.

### F.8 Pitot-static truth table (for W31)

| Blockage                    | Airspeed                                  | Altimeter     | VSI                          |
| --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ------------- | ---------------------------- |
| Pitot ram only (drain open) | Drops to zero                             | Normal        | Normal                       |
| Pitot ram and drain         | Acts like an altimeter (rises in a climb) | Normal        | Normal                       |
| Static port                 | Reads low in a climb, high in a descent   | Frozen        | Zero                         |
| Alternate static in use     | Slightly high                             | Slightly high | Momentary climb, then normal |

### F.9 Standard-rate and timed turns

```
standard rate = 3°/s → 360° in 2 min; 45° in 15 s
bank for standard rate ≈ TAS ÷ 10 + 7       (v1 G.5)
```

### F.10 Rules of thumb (label as such)

- Gust additive: + ½ the gust factor.
- Tailwind: +10% takeoff distance per 2 kt; headwind: −10% per 9 kt (⚠ verify POH notes).
- Dry grass: +15% ground roll (⚠ verify).
- Takeoff abort: ~70% of lift-off speed by the runway midpoint (⚠ verify source).
- Night reserve: 45 min; IFR reserve: 45 min after the alternate.

### What you (the project owner) must do personally

1. **Set up the email provider and domain** (Phase 14):
   1. Create an account at the provider you chose (Resend: <https://resend.com>, or
      Postmark: <https://postmarkapp.com>).
   2. Add your sending domain in the provider's dashboard ("Domains" → "Add domain").
   3. Copy the DNS records it shows (SPF, DKIM, and optionally DMARC) into your domain
      registrar's DNS settings; wait for the provider to show "Verified".
   4. Create an API key with "send" permission only; add it to Render as
      `EMAIL_API_KEY`, plus `EMAIL_PROVIDER` and `EMAIL_FROM` (Appendix E).
2. **Upgrade the database when needed** (Phase 16 or 26): in Atlas → your cluster →
   "Edit configuration" → choose M10 → confirm; then enable "Cloud Backup". Take a
   snapshot before the first v2 migration.
3. **Fly the sim.** Every variant of every v2 challenge, at night, in the mountains and in
   IMC (Sections 7, 51). Only you can do this.
4. **Record flight-test data** for the performance tables (Section 7.1) and electrical
   endurance (Section 7.6).
5. **Download current charts and plates** and choose the IFR procedures (Section 12.4).
6. **Take the screenshots** in Sections 46.4 and 47.4.
7. **Try VATSIM yourself** before publishing L15.5 (Section 7.7).
8. **Recruit beta testers**, ideally including an instrument-rated pilot or CFII
   (Phase 25).
9. **Review the legal updates** (Section 54) and the privacy policy changes.
10. **Make the judgement calls** in the Decision Log (Section 5), especially D2-04
    (companion timing), D2-05 (regions) and the Atlas upgrade timing.
